# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1981

## The Meaning of Vyāsa Pūjā by Bhakti Caru Swami

*Pūjā* means “worship,” and Vyāsa, or Śrīla Vyāsadeva, is the compiler and supreme authority on the Vedic literature. Vyāsa Pūjā, literally meaning “the worship of Vyāsa,” refers to the worship of the genuine spiritual master, who is the ideal representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, on the occasion of his appearance day.

The spiritual master is the representative if Śrīla Vyāsadeva because he is distributing the nectar that Śrīla Vyāsadeva churned out of the ocean of the *Vedas. Veda* means “knowledge,” the knowledge of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In *Bhagavad-gītā,* Kṛṣṇa says, *vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyah:* “By all the *Vedas* I am to be known.” The ultimate objective of the *Vedas* is to reveal the identity of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. And that Vedic revelation becomes manifest in the heart of the conditioned soul by the mercy of the spiritual master because he has been specially empowered by the Lord to bestow that mercy. That is why the spiritual master is the most worthy representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the original distributor of the *Vedas.*

*Oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya:* “We are born in the darkest ignorance. Although we had eyes, we couldn’t see. Whatever we saw was nothing but a reflection of reality—eternal, spiritual reality—and we mistook that reflection to be real. We were living in a world of dim reflections, and all our experiences were nothing but dreams. Our spiritual master opened our eyes with the torch of knowledge, the knowledge about the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is the cause of all causes and the source of everything. *Tasmai Śrī-gurave namah:* “We offer our respectful obeisances to the lotus feet of that spiritual master.” Our self-surrender unto him out of heartfelt gratitude is our spontaneous worship of his lotus feet.

The spiritual master reveals transcendental knowledge within our hearts. Kṛṣṇa’s identity is revealed. His transcendental pastimes are revealed, His eternal relationship with His associates is revealed, and we get a sublime taste of absolute knowledge and eternal bliss. He guides us to the spiritual sky and satisfies the deepest longings of the soul. This causes our heart to melt in the fire of gratitude and compels us to surrender completely unto his lotus feet. This offering of the self in spontaneous dedication is the true worship of the spiritual master, the Vyāsā Pūjā.

The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, is sitting in everyone’s heart as the Supersoul, waiting for the living entity to turn toward Him and recognize Him as his dearmost friend.

*Kona bhagye kona jīvera śraddhā yadi haya:* “Out of sheer good fortune, when someone turns his face toward Kṛṣṇa and develops the faith that He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead…” *Tabe sei jīva sādhusaṅga je karaya:* “… then Kṛṣṇa arranges for Him to associate with the *sādhus,* the devotees.” *Sādhu-saṅga haite haya śravaṇa kīrtana:* “In the association of devotees, he hears about Kṛṣṇa and gradually starts to comprehend His glories.” The association with the pure devotee enchants him, and he feels very much drawn toward the bona fide spiritual master. He feels as if his *guru* has stolen his heart away. He can’t help but love him with all his heart and soul, and in that love he offers himself as the sacrifice at his lotus feet. As the external manifestation of the Supersoul, the genuine spiritual master appears before us so that we can see him with our material sense; but his form is not material, his words are not material, and he has nothing to do with the material nature. He appears here simply out of Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy, the bona fide spiritual master becomes perceivable to our material senses, simply to spiritualize our hearts to become submerged in the ocean of transcendental bliss. He tells us, “Do not hesitate to depend on Kṛṣṇa. Just surrender to Him and He’ll give you everything you need.”

Trusting the spiritual master, we surrender more and more, and as our surrender increases, we are increasingly drawn toward him. We renounce our material identity, and in return we achieve the highest benedictions: the rejuvenation of our lost relationship with the Supreme Self, ŚrĪ Kṛṣṇa.

The external manifestation of the Supersoul, the bona fide spiritual master, takes us by the hand and teaches us how to walk on the platform. Then, one day, just as he appeared to us out of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, he disappears from our limited vision out of that same causeless mercy. Though no longer physically present, he watches how much we are surrendering ourselves unto him. Silently, he says, “When I was with you, you used to say so eloquently how much you love me. Now is the time for you to prove your love for me. Real love flourishes in the fire of separation. Now, prove that your surrender to me was not impelled by material motives but inspired by love that fills the heart with resolute determination to follow my orders. Do not deceive yourself, thinking that I am gone. I instructed you not to trust your senses. So do not trust your senses, and think that I am gone. If you love me, then find out where I am; and thus you will attain to the kingdom of Kṛṣṇa, Goloka Vṛndāvana.

“Do not deviate from following my instructions as they are. Do not try to concoct some meaning out of them simply to suit your need. Don’t make my instructions a convenience for your sense gratification. Become free from the bondage of matter. Otherwise, how can you go back to Godhead? Unless you are totally surrendered, unconditionally, the gates to the spiritual sky will not be open for you. Kṛṣṇa will judge you according to your submission to me, because I am His representative. Your scholarship or your expertise will not be of any use unless they are surrendered to Him, through me, with unalloyed devotion and intense dedication.

“By Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement, I appeared before you in my physical from and made your aware that my instructions are nondifferent form me. In my instructions, I am unchanging and eternal. That is where you will find me. If you love me, then come associate with my instructions, embrace them with all sincerity, and make them your very existence. If you want to worship me, then let this be the standard. I do not need the flowers that are offered without love, nor the food that is prepared without devotion. I want your very self as an offering. And that is how you worship the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. That is the meaning of Vyāsa Pūjā.”

Bhakti Cāru Swami

## Introduction by Mukunda dāsa

And then all the devotees began to cry…but we were still chanting. We were chanting “ Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda…Prabhupada has gone back to Vaikuṇṭha, and …we felt Prabhupāda had entered everyone’s heart. He had become closer than he had ever been to us.

This moment of our beloved *gurudev’s* disappearance form this world on November 14,1977 will remain forever in our memory. And with each successive edition of this Śrī Vyāsā Pūjā offering, as we attempt to glorify him more and more, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s presence grows deeper within our hearts.

Śrīla Prabhupāda inspired us to attain continual remembrance of him when he wrote of his spiritual master, “I still consider His Divine Grace to be always with me by his *vāṇī,* his words.”

What are Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books emphasized preaching. In his *Caitanya-cariṭamṛta* purports he writes, “It is the duty of every disciple …to spread Lord Caitanya’s message al over the world… preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, therefore his sincere devotees must carry out his desire… Śrī Caitanya’s devotional cult teaches one not to sit down in one place but to spread the devotional cult all over the world.”

But Śrīla Prabhupāda did not just order us to go out and preach. He trained us to compete for his transcendental ambitions. And when by his grace we achieved one of his cherished goals, Śrīla Prabhupāda sometimes glorified us, but he also made it clear that his praise did not come cheaply.

A case in point occurred in the summer of 1972. Many senior devotees were sitting with Śrīla Prabhupāda in his Los Angeles quarters. Suddenly, Viśāla, one of ISCKON’s leading book distributors, burst into the room and offered his obeisances. Śrīla Prabhupāda smiled from ear to ear at his disciple, whose face was bright red from hundreds of hours of *saṅkīrtana* in the southern California sun. Vicāla sat up, “I distributed 108 books today, Śrīla Prabhupāda.” His spiritual master’s eyes opened wide. “*Ooooh,”* he intoned approvingly. The other devotees murmured about this being a new world record. “Very good,” said Śrīla Prabhupāda. Then Viśāla continued, “One thing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to go to India.” Śrīla Prabhupāda: “You can go anywhere. You are Nārada Muni.” Everyone was stunned. Viśāla was embarrassed. A brief pause ensued and the shock wave subsided. Composing himself, Viśāla looked up at Śrīla Prabhupāda in earnest. “Śrīla Prabhupāda, the one thing I need and want most are your blessings,” to which Śrīla Prabhupāda replied, “Distribute more books, and I’ll give you more blessings.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda relentlessly pushed us on to new heights. In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* he wrote, “If one is true to Gaura-Nitāi’s service in disciplic succession, he can even excel Nityananda Prabhu’s service….Nityananda Prabhu delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi, but a servant of Nityanand can deliver many thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis. That is the spiritual benediction of the disciplic succession….” In this purport to *Madhya-līlā* 16.65, Śrīla Prabhupāda goes on to write of Arjuna winning the battle of Kurukṣetra, of Hanumān jumping across the sea and building a bridge of floating stones, and of “even more difficult tasks that can be preformed by the preachers remaining faithful to the service of the Lord.”

The *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* states that under the guidance of the expert spiritual master “the whole world can be turned into the spiritual abode.” But even making this planet Kṛṣṇa conscious—even the entire universe—would not be the end. The full range of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s vision will always remain beyond our comprehension.

But his mercy will always be within reach through devotion. Therefore we humbly pray that Śrīla Prabhupāda will kindly accept these VYĀSA PŪJĀ offerings and be pleased upon us.

Mukunda dāsa.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the G.B.C.

## Ādi Keśava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. This most insignificant servant begs to submit a humble offering at your feet

I dreamed we set out to sea last night, And saved us from rebirth.  
Beneath a rising moon; As if to prove we’re not alone  
The waves reflected golden light You’ve come to take these wanderers  
With froth of silver hue; home.  
The helmsman’s steady hand assured  
That landfall we would see; Columbus sailed to find the East  
My troubled heart he gently lured And stumbled on this land;  
With tales of ‘cross the sea. We’ve traded liberation’s peace  
No more would I be forced to roam For Raman Ret’s sands;  
In search of my eternal home. But still the sirens beckon me  
To relish what is dead:  
The seas began to shift and roll Oh holiest desire tree  
As storm winds whipped the sheets; Please shade the path we tread.  
To keep on course the crewmen toiled Within my heart this seed you’ve sown  
As waves crashed at their feet; O Prabhupada, please take me home.  
In mortal fear we turned our heads  
And asked how we’d be saved; I stand exposed of shameless pride  
“Just chant the holy name,” you said, Ambitious for distinction;  
“And spurn this watery grave.” An apaRādhā this fool will glide  
We marveled how our faith had grown To spiritual extinction;  
In your desire to take us home. O Prabhupada, please guide me through  
The ocean of illusion;  
We joined you in the kīrtana Mahāprabhu has given you  
Which became a full-throat roar; The Bhagavata’s conclusion.  
The stormy seas began to calm Take me as one whose life you own  
The deluge ceased to pour; Aborad your ship that’s headed home.  
As sun rays shone on Vraja’s shores  
Your eyes were filled with mirth; Begging to remain always in your service,

*The squall had swept our pride o’erboard*  
Ādi Keśava Swami

## Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for confirming in so many ways that you approve my humble insignificant service at your lotus feet. Although I have not deserved this, you mercifully know that without this confirmation I would have died. Now when I look at your glowing divine face, I see your beautiful lotus smile.

Thank you for so much opportunity to serve your numerous devotees.

*Begging you for qualification to properly serve your devotees,*  
Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

## Balavanta dāsa

**The Desire Tree**

Stumbling along through the dark stormy evening,  
Now and again there’s a flashing of lightning  
Filling the sky and rumbling the earth  
While I march ever onward to a repeating birth.

I strain and I labor, I hope for the day,  
I struggle and battle along this difficult way.  
There’s no goal for me though, it’s only survival  
By just keeping alive I’m functioning quite well.

It is not to say I don’t have my dreams,  
My hopes, my illusions, my boots and bluejeans,  
But they’ll all be lost to tide and to time  
While others will soon claim all I sought was mine.

But then a fine tree not far from my way,  
Covered with fruits and flowers like May  
Invites for a rest and break from the rain  
Prasādam, the Gītā, the Lord’s holy name.

Oh, this tree’s not common, it’s quite rare indeed.  
I’ll spend the whole spring here, it fills all my needs,  
And in came the summer followed by fall,  
When winter arrived the tree had grown tall.

The tree is unique though, don’t judge it by seasons;  
Don’t try to explain it by material reasons.  
Transplanted from heaven, it always will live;  
It has not come to take but only to give.

Balavanta dāsa

## Bhagavān Goswami

The varṇa-saṅkara amassed through negation,  
Peaking the new wave of Kaliṣ invasion  
Not knowing what to be done or not done,  
Blindly watching the setting sun—  
With one eye lust the other greed  
Their heart enriched with Kali’s seed.

With no idea of why they are born,  
What they are—-where they come from,  
No clue of perfection in their paltry sojourn  
Of chewing the chewed and tasting the thorn.

They have somehow or other taken the place  
Of those who know best for the human race.  
But clearly etched across their face  
Their masterpiece of the soul’s disgrace.

O Lord, how long have we waited for him  
Our master and lord, Your most intimate friend  
To lift us out of this ocean of sin  
And give us the vision of life’s perfection?

Like Vāmana you came and covered the earth,  
Your steps put an end to death and birth  
For those who could put your dust on their head  
You bought them back from the living dead.

Your books you placed upon the scale,  
Your test to see where faith was frail  
Against the weight of Kali’s power  
Your assurance sweet as the lotus flower.

Our divine master, Gairāṅga’s ray,  
Would you call for us at the end of the day  
To hear if the battle was going our way  
If everyone was still willing to stay.

Someday we will see your vision unfold  
Of a world where devotees are taken as gold,  
One day they will see how your mercy was showered  
How your love saved the world in its darkest hour.

Bhagavān Goswami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*om ajnana-timirandhasya jnananjana-salakaya*  
*caksur unmilitam yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namah*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most blessed event, kindly allow me to express the following few words before the assembled Vaiṣṇavas. Our most beloved master and spiritual father, His Divine Grace Paramahaṁsa Parivrājjakācārya Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada Maharaja has brought all of us together. Our bond is our love for him. Śrīla Prabhupāda transformed all of us totally, inside and outside. His only desire was to make everyone a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. He wanted to demonstrate an ideal example of a noble and civilized human society so that everyone could take up *nivṛtti-mārga,* the path of renunciation. He sacrificed everything and gave us *sanātana-dharma.* He gave us the matchless gift, *golokera prema-dhana,* the treasure wealth of *prema* from Goloka-dhāma, in the form of ISKCON. He explained to each of us that modern men are rushing to the darkest corner of hellish existence and that the mission of ISKCON is to save all of them unconditionally. He gave that responsibility to all of us collectively, a great responsibility, indeed!

The Vedic verdict is *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.* “Traverse the trail which your previous *ācārya* has passed.” It is a great opportunity and a great day for all of us to focus on this point very carefully. Śrīla Prabhupāda was very much concerned for our well-being, for our cooperation among each other, and for the proper maintenance and management of ISKCON—-right up until the moment of his departure. He has left many specific instructions with clear directions to facilitate the preaching in all frontiers. For example, his instruction on management is given in the purport of *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* (7.13.34) as “A *sannyāsī* should have an institution meant to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness; he need not accumulate money for himself. We recommend that as soon as money is accumulated in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, fifty percent of it should be invested for printing books, and fifty percent for expenditures, especially in establishing centers all over the world. The managers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement should be extremely cautious in regard to this point. Otherwise money will be the cause of lamentation, illusion, fear, material attachment, material poverty, and unnecessary hard work. When I was alone in Vṛndāvana, I never attempted to construct *maṭhas* or temples; rather, I was fully satisfied with the small amount of money I could gather by selling *Back to Godhead,* and thus I would provide for myself and also print the literature. When I went to foreign countries, I lived according to the same principle, but when Europeans and Americans began to give money profusely, I started temples and Deity worship. The same principle should still be followed. Whatever money is collected should be spent for Kṛṣṇa, and not a farthing for sense gratification. This is the *Bhāgavata* principle.”

Today is the day when we can all contemplate on how to increase our collective effort to push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Today is the day when we can when we should focus our attention on how to improve the quality of our movement in all aspects, following the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas.* Today is the day for contemplating on what Śrīla Prabhupāda has done for each of us and for comparing our lives today to what is was like before we met Śrīla Prabhupāda. Today is the day when we can contemplate how to assist our elder Godbrothers and how to accommodate the feelings of the younger. This will bring the proper mood and understanding. Today is also the day when we reemphasize the fact that the concepts of “my zone,” “my men,” “my project,” and so on, do not supercede the collective *saṅkīrtana* spirit. Such concepts are simply functional as they are like the different organs of a large body. If each organ wants to “do his own thing,” the whole body suffers. We should realize that a single organ does not represent the whole body.

Everyone says, “I am following what Śrīla Prabhupāda instructed me to do.” Maybe everyone is doing that. But ours is a *saṅkīrtana* movement, meaning we have to glorify together. Thus, a single individual does not represent ISKCON. All together we represent ISKCON, and ISKCON is Prabhupada. So, now is the time to find ways to solidify the *saṅkīrtana* force. Let us follow the principle that Śrīla Prabhupada established so that we can be stronger in our unity. Any other separate interest will simply add pain to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s body, ISKCON. In spite of great difficulties, Śrīla Prabhupāda was constantly trying to bring unity among his Godbrothers. The living example of this was his formation, just before his departure to Goloka-dhāma, of the Bhaktivedanta Swami Charity Trust. This trust includes five of his disciples and two of his Godbrothers and is meant to refurbish the *gaura-maṇdala-bhūmi.* Prabhupada told us that we would make him greatly happy by doing this.

The glories of the master are judged by the qualities of his disciples. Let the whole world know that we are the worthy disciples and grand-disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is our duty to make an all-out effort to make Śrīla Prabhupāda the most famous personality on earth. Our glory should depend on how much we labor and on how much we sacrifice to make Prabhupada’s mission, ISKCON, most famous and honored. Let this be our common foundation of unity. There is no need to blame anyone for whatever mistakes we may have committed in the past. Whatever has happened must all be due to the inconceivable mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This is to remind us how great Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee is. This is to remind us how insignificant and helpless we are in the absence of the master. We have all become orphans. When the father departs untimely, it is quite natural to expect some symptoms of difficulty in the middle of unbearable agony of separation from our divine master. But we have a great task to fulfill. We have to bring this *bhāgavata-dharma* to every door all over the world. We have to make sure that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s name is known and glorified in every home on the planet for thousands and thousands of generations to come. We have to show that ISKCON is *glolkera prema-dhana* to the whole world. We have to show to the whole world that ISKCON is *cintāmaṇi,* touchstone. We have to show that we are Vaisnavas. We have to show that a Vaisnava is he who knows the absolute values of life, meaning the individual *jiva* is an eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa, *jīvera svarūpa haya kṛṣṇera nitya dāsa.* We have to show that we are Vaisnavas. We have nothing to do with the temporary designations. Our identity is not G.B.C., *sannyāsī, gṛhastha,* and so on. We are eternal servants of the servants of the Supreme Lord. Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu gave this instruction very clearly, and Śrīla Prabhupāda taught us the same principle. Our business is just to follow these principles, *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.*

We have to smash the material philosophy of life widely propagated by the foolish materialists. We have to establish the absolute fact that life is *sac-cid-ānanda,* which can be understood only by following the process: *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā amāniṇā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ.* Let everyone know the unparalleled qualities of the Vaisnavas through the devotees of ISKCON. Let these qualities bloom with full fragrance in every corner of the globe. Let the whole world feel that ISKCON is greatly needed. Let everyone be attracted to the sweet aroma coming from the transcendental garden of ISKCON. Let the whole world know that Śrīla Prabhupāda is the most expert gardener who planted, nurtured and cared for all these tiny, tiny flower plants in the form of his disciples to offer at the feet of his divine Guru Maharaja, our divine grandfather Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Prabhupada, at the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and at the lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Let everyone know that the mission of human life is to be a devotee of Kṛṣṇa through ISKCON. Let every parent be proud of his child being in ISKCON. Let millions sing and dance to the tune of *golokera prema-dhana hari-nāma saṅkīrtana,* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Hare Hare / Hare Rama Hare Rama, Rama Rama Hare Hare. Let everyone listen to the song of Narottama dāsaThākura: *manusya-janama pāiyā, rādhā-Kṛṣṇa nā bhajiyā jāniyā śuniyā biṣa khāinu,* “In the human form of life if one does not serve and glorify Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, one is knowingly drinking poison.” We have to broadcast at the top of our voice that everyone must get out of *māyā,* subtle or gross. We have this *cintāmaṇi* gift. Let us give it freely to everyone. This is what Nārada Muni did. This is what Caitanya Mahāprabhu demonstrated. This is what Śrīla Prabhupāda taught us. We have to follow, *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ.*

ISKCON is most unique in the history of man’s civilization, especially in Kali-yuga. The platform of unity and harmony, irrespective of caste, creed, color, name, fame, prestige, position, nationality, and so on, is only possible in ISKCON. Let everyone see that ISKCON has the mystical potency to bring everyone together. The world should know that ours is based on *sanātana-dharma.* Ours is based on *bhakti-yoga.* Ours is based on Absolute Truth, the truth that remains true beyond time and space, beyond all boundaries. Ours is based on *paramparā.* It is the topmost transcendental platform. Let the United Nations’ complete nonsense ideology be totally dissolved in the transparent absolute solution of ISKCON. Let all the members of the U.N. join happily the platform established by ISKCON. Let the whole world be transformed into Vaikuṇṭḥa. Only ISKCON can do this, and Śrīla Prabhupāda ordered us to do it. So we have a great responsibility to carry out.

The position of our beloved divine master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is unparalleled in the history of spiritual revolution. He transformed the whole world in a decade’s time. Prabhupada transformed the heart of everyone who had the chance to see him. He gave the most wonderful gift to every educational institution of the world in the form of his literatures. He was the most expert alchemist, who picked the rejected pebbles and transformed them into the most valuable gems. He was a champion in every aspect of transcendental activities. It was Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī who expressed about Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu saying *namo mahā-vadānyāya Kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te kṛṣṇāya Kṛṣṇa-caitanya-nāmne gaura-tviṣe namaḥ.* “Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya is more magnanimous than any other *avatāra,* even Kṛṣṇa himself, because He is bestowing freely what no one else has ever given—pure love of Kṛṣṇa.” I can very humbly express that Śrīla Prabhupāda is even more magnanimous than Lord Caitanya because he was the one who picked up even the most rotten souls, like myself, and gave us freely *golokera prema-dhana,* which no one could do before. He gave us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the most confidential knowledge. It was he who crossed the oceans and mountains sacrificing all personal comfort sin such an advanced age to distribute freely this *golokera prema dhana* which no one could do before. He gave us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the most confidential knowledge. It was he who crossed the oceans and mountains sacrificing all personal comforts in such an advanced age to distribute freely this *golokera prema dhana* to every one all over the globe. He was compassion personified. It was he who followed in the footsteps of Narāda Muni; it was he who followed in the footprints of Prahlāda Maharaja; it was he who fulfilled the predictions of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu; it was he who followed in the footsteps of Śrīla Rūpa, Sanātana, Raghunātha dāsa, Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa, Jīva, and Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmīs; it was he who fulfilled the predictions of Bhaktivinoda Thākura; it was he who carried out the orders of his Guru Maharaja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Prabhupada, without the slightest tinge of personal motivation. His unlimited glories cannot be described properly by any form of expression. Śrīla Prabhupāda is *mahā-mahā-bhāgavata.* Śrīla Prabhupāda is *mahā-paramahaṁsa.* Had Śrīla Prabhupāda not appeared among us there would have been no chance for this most lowly self to even dream of expressing these few words.

Prabhupada had so much love for all of us. He was greatly concerned for our advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We knew his mood well. We are all his dear children. So I have a right to claim that we are all brothers and sisters because this is what I have learned from Śrīla Prabhupāda. So, brothers and sisters, on this most auspicious day of the divine appearance of our most beloved Gurudeva, let us try to forget whatever mistakes we may have made in the past. Let us all come together, let us fall at the lotus feet of our divine father, let us not try to find fault with each other, let us try to help each other. Let us try whatever means we have to please Śrīla Prabhupāda to the utmost. So, let us print a lot of books, distribute a lot of books, establish a lot of centers all over the world, and let us flood the whole world with Śrīla Prabhupāda’s message, and let us make the year 1986, the 500the anniversary of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the most glorious year in the history of the Caitanya era. This would be most pleasing to Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Jagat-guru* Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

*Your most lowly servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhavānanda Goswami

I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda and pray that my memory of him may never diminish but remain strong, solidly fixing me in service to him. Śrīla Prabhupāda was a “personalist to the end.” As such eh was a sublime composite of so many personal activities which, although they would appear ordinary in a mundaner, became wonderful when performed by him and served to entangle me more and more in a net of love for him.

Such as when he rubbed by head at the airport in L.A. in 1969 jut like a forgiving father.  
When he rubbed by head at the airport in Delhi in 1977 just like a forgiving father.  
When he told me his original color was reddish-gold.  
How his deep brown eyes were always moist with tears.  
How his nostrils flares when he would smell incense.  
How he would suddenly sit erect during massage, revealing a strong youthful body.  
How his legs were unexpectedly strong and powerfully built.  
How he told me that when he was a young man he was well built and ate *halavā* every day.  
How he would softly sing a line or two from the poetry of Narottama dāsa or Bhaktivinoda when taking massage.  
How he picked up my bead bag, which I had foolishly left on the floor, and hung it on a doorknob saying I should be more careful with my beads.  
How the soles of his feet and palms of his hand were rosy pink.  
How he taught me to brush my teeth with the twig from a *nima* tree.  
How he never scolded my despite my foolishness.  
How he always referred tome to others as “our Bhavānanda.”  
How he always had the scent of sandalwood.  
How he drank water without touching the glass to his lips.  
How he broke a piece off the *capātī* and scooped up the vegetable with one hand.  
How he carefully watched me wash the marble floors of his quarters in Bombay in the heat of the summer afternoon.  
How he made a little eyepatch soaked in turmeric juice for his eye.  
How he always carried the keys to his *alamāris* with him wherever he went, even to the bath.  
How he deeply, warmly, protectingly, and personally loved me.  
How he allowed me to massage him, bathe him, dry him, dress him, feed him, put him to rest, wake him, garland him, anoint him with sandal paste, wash for him, read to him, sit with him be with him and love him.  
Even at the risk of being called sentimental, what can I do; Śrīla Prabhupāda, memories of you are all I am made of!

*I beg to remain your eternal servant in the dust of your lotus feet,*  
Bhavānanda Goswami

## Brahmānanda Swami

We take this opportunity of the most blessed even, the appearance day of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, to reaffirm our unswerving devotion to his mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. We are convinced that only by Kṛṣṇa consciousness can all over the world’s unending problems be solved on an individual and collective basis. His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda was so expert that he introduced Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that we how are so unqualified could understand it, and he mercifully gave us his association, even though by our blunders we caused him inconveniences. Now he has entrusted us to carry on this great task of saving the world, which has gone mad due to excessive sense gratification.

I pray for his mercy to enthuse me with the determination to act as he acted, not to imitate in a cheap way, but to follow in the footprints of his divine lotus feet to purify myself and then to purify others, so that pure love of God, Kṛṣṇa, will manifest in the heart. I then will be able to again reunite myself in his divine association in the spiritual world, far beyond this material world of pain and pleasure, where he eternally resides, participating in pastimes of bliss, with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Śrīla Prabhupāda was always very kind upon me, so I am sure that he will not deny me this, my heart’s desire. Who else is there who is such a powerful transcendental personality, who can take the most unqualified and elevate them to the kingdom of God? Therefore, I worship His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda and never tire of speaking of his glories to anyone or everyone I meet. I pray for the day when the world will come to recognize the exalted position of Śrīla Prabhupāda and join with me to bow down to the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, and also to his successor *gurus,* because on that day the world will be saved from its certain present hellish course of godlessness and suffering.

In my opinion, Śrīla Prabhupāda is understood to be an *āveśa-avatāra,* an incarnation of the Lord, as is proved by the following verse:

*Sei jiva nija-bhakti karena prakāśe*  
*tāhāra darśane ‘vaiṣnava’haya sarva-deśe*

**“Thus He empowered living beings \[His pure devotees\] by manifesting in them so much of His own devotion that people in all other countries become devotees by seeing them.” (C.c.** ***Antya*** **2.14)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda is not an ordinary person like us, but fully endowed with the powers of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It is my great fortune to be his eternal servant.  
Brahmānanda Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

I beg to offer my most respectful obeisances at the divine lotus feet of my most exalted, eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Visṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada. As I sit down to write this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I find myself blank. I feel that due to my contamination, I lack the ability to praise you. Your achievements and glories are impossible to describe. I have no qualification to make an offering to the *jagad-guru.*

The ocean of this material world is tossing. Day by day things are deteriorating, confirming the prediction made by the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* five thousand years ago:

*Tataś cānudinaṁdharmah*  
*Satyaṁ śaucaṁ kṣamā dayā*  
*kālena balinā rājan*  
*naṅkṣyaty āyur balaṁ smṛtiḥ*

**“My dear king, with each day religion, truthfulness, cleanliness, forgiveness, mercy, duration of life, bodily strength, and memory will all decrease more and more by the mighty force of time.” (*****Bhāg.*** **12.2.1)**

The only hope for suffering humanity is Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the entire Brahma-gauḍīya-sampradāya there has never been a spiritual master as great as you. No one has succeeded in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness so widely to so many people in such a short period of time. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu established Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the rest of the world to Your Divine Grace. You are the mercy incarnation of the Supreme Lord. You are like a rain cloud, which pours rain water everywhere. Whoever you met was captured by your sincerity. You worked tirelessly to save humanity. Feeling compassion upon the fallen souls, you worked round the clock to establish the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and translate the Vedic texts into English. You have created a sun by which those suffering from the dense darkness of ignorance can see the light. Your Bhaktivedanta purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam, Bhagavad-gītā,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* are giving light to the entire world.

Just like it is impossible to fully describe the transcendental glories of the Lord, it is also impossible to fully describe your glories. What you have done is unrivaled in human history. All alone at an old age, you created a spiritual revolution, which even after your departure, from this mortal world, is expanding day by day. You successfully transplanted the ancient Vedic instructions to the demoniac Western society. Once when someone asked you if you could do any magic you replied, “My magic has been that I have converted so many *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas.”* Getting the young Westerners to give up *mlecchas* habits like illicit sex; meat, fish, and eggs; gambling; and intoxication was no small magic. No *yogī* could have achieved this feat in Kali-yuga. Further, you are able to make the spiritual revolution without making any spiritual compromises. Because of your purity you were able to conquer over the whole world all alone. Your secret to success was complete dependence upon the Supreme Lord and your eternal, divine spiritual master. Maharaja Yudhiṣṭhira was helped by his brothers; Lord Rāmacandra was helped by Hanumān and his army of monkeys; and Lord Caitanya was assisted by Lord Nityaṇanda and the other members of the Pañca-tattva in establishing *dharma.* But you were assisted by no one. Even your Godbrothers in the Gauḍīya Maṭha refused to assist you. You had faith in Lord Caitanya’s prediction:

*pṛthīvite āche yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

And hence you continued to endeavor to realize your spiritual master’s instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most powerful, eternal, and confidential associate of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Any ability that I may have to bring someone to Kṛṣṇa consciousness is just because of you. My position is like that of a “sold-out animal,” who is completely dependent upon the master in every respect. You are my supreme, eternal master and have given me everything. You gave me the medicine by which the suffering humanity can be cured—the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra.*

On this auspicious day, I would like to once again express my love and gratitude to Your Divine Grace. I would like to reaffirm my full commitment to assist you in bringing Lord Caitanya’s teachings to every town and village on this planet. I pray to you to give me the strength to execute your instructions. The knowledge that you have left behind in your Bhaktivedanta purports will guide humanity for the next ten thousand years. I pray that I may be able to help expand the distribution of your books, which alone are capable of giving light to those entrapped in darkness.

*Your most fallen servant,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Haṁsadūta Swami

**Prabhupada**

Prabhupada Prabhupada  
Sent by God Where are you?  
Prabhupada Prabhupada  
I am a clod What to do?  
Prabhupada Prabhupada  
It’s so hard Take me too  
Prabhupada Prabhupada  
Just a nod Give a clue  
Prabhupada Prabhupada  
Earth you trod Left your shoes  
Prabhupada Prabhupada  
I’m so sad You are true  
Prabhupada Prabhupada  
Gone to God Guru of Gurus
