## Baltimore

The Gītā, sung by Bhagavān,  
Proclaims that He’s the only one,  
Complete in beauty, wealth, and strength,  
Fame, detachment, and intelligence.

Pure bhaktas, Kṛṣṇa’s favorite sons,  
Sometimes are also “Bhagavan.”  
Prabhupada, like Nārada, at your feet,  
We find six opulences, complete.

Beautiful Prabhupada, enchanting smiles,  
Cross your face Vaikuntha style.

What rich man claims, on every shore,  
Palaces like yours?—-and servants galore.

‘Though other strong men conquer lands,  
you’ve conquered the senses’ strong demands.

You’ve humbly spread Lord Kṛṣṇa’s name,  
To every door; that’s your fame.

An ISKCON kingdom you patiently built,  
And renounced at once when Kṛṣṇa willed.

But not until all knowledge known  
Was put by you in grantha form.  
(Now in your absence we see the dawn,  
a day of wisdom, your books the sun.)

All opulence you’ve clearly shown,  
(unlike the bogus ‘Bhagavāns.’)  
“Bhagavan?” you said, “Not me. In fact,  
I’m Bhagavan dāsa—and aspiring, at that!”

If you’re not Bhagavan, you’re proof,  
That Kṛṣṇa is, for He sent you,  
To preach His Gītā in this dark place.  
Enlight’ning all, Your Divine Grace.

*Prabhupada’s disciples and granddisciples of ISKCON Baltimore,*

Kākakaṇṭha dāsa / Akhiladhāra dāsa / Uddhava dāsa / Rasaprada dāsa / Laulyam dāsa / Guru-Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Viṣṇu-jvara dāsa / Divya-jñāna dāsa / Prahlāda dāsa / Bhakta Steve / Bhakta Jeff / Bhakta Mark / Mr. And Mrs. Jagadish Patel / Ravi Singh / Nanda-patnī-devī dāsī / Mahāvegvatī-devī dāsī / Hlādinī-śaktī-devī dāsī / Devahūti-devī dāsī / Rohiṇī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Jamie / Bhaktin Lynn

## Bangalore

*Dearest Prabhupada,*

With tear-dampened eyes we beg to be able to offer our *sahasra-koṭi-daṇḍavats* in the shadow of the dust raised by your passing *pāda’s* while you lead the lost back to Godhead.

Whenever the chanting resounds the holy name of Śacīnandana, wherever the cymbals chime, accompanied by dancing in time, we beg to remain because there is Your Divine Grace.

You have created an empire which can never crumble, which will not fade, oblivious to the waves of time, your immortal *vānī.* The very same timeless wisdom has been imparted to the heart of Brahmā, upon his lotus seat. The very same narration was spoken by Nārada to Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa on the banks of the Sarasvatī at Śamyāprāsa in Badarikāśrama. The very same message was written by the tusk of tumili of lord Gaṇeṣa. The very same message has come from Your Divine Grace by countless painstaking hours of tireless realizations.

“The great sage, who was fully equipped in knowledge could see, through his transcendental vision, the deterioration of everything material due to the influence of the age. He could see that the faithless people in general would be reduced in duration of life and would be impatient due to lack of goodness. Thus he contemplated for the welfare of men in all statuses and orders of life.” (*Bhāg.* 1.4.17-18*)*

The glue used in binding, the ink used by the press, the string, the paper, the *vāṇī,* all are of the same spiritual nature, eternal and sublime. The literary classics of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the pillar of ISKCON’s success, the illuminating sun, release for the masses from the ‘dance of death,’ the macabre of *saṁsāra.* Your books are the basis. Distribution of books is the essence. The ways and means are the principle. The same *yajña* is the force.

If and until every town and village, every home, every living entity has received your literary gift, incarnation of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, we beg to remain at your feet. When this tiny planet had been delivered, allow us interplanetary passage on your feet to the next and the next, on and on throughout the *brahmāṇḍa brahmite,* life past life, universe through universe. This one-fourth fragment of the totality of creation should and will be delivered to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa in the eternal abode by the saving power of your books. Just please one thing, Gurudeva, allow me to carry your bookbag through the whole *līlā-*marathon of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana-yajña* as you distribute the riches from the storehouse of the holy name of Lord Hari.

I have no intelligence, my mind is full of faults. You are the master, I the *śūdra.* Just let me carry the bag. Hare Kṛṣṇa, *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga,* transcendental book distribution, Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya jaya jaya!!!*

*The Vaisnava-kukkuras of Bangalore Yatra,*

Bhakti-svarūpa Swami / Tattva-darśa dāsa / Rāsānanda dāsa / Godruma dāsa / Tapana dāsa / Kṛṣṇacandra dāsa / Ācārya dāsa / Nitāipāda-kamala dāsa / Nitya-Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Kāmākhya dāsa / Madhusūdana dāsa / Viśvāmitra dāsa / Ārādhana-devī dāsī / Maha-Lakṣmī-devī-dasi

## Bangkok

Since time immemorial for billions of years  
This illusion became a fertile ground  
Māyādevīhad trapped the conditioned souls;  
Amazingly real like a mirage she appears  
The enchanting actress, now smiling, now in tears  
Casting her spell and skillfully playing her role  
Leaving the audience, now delighted, now in fears  
Helplessly moved as iron dust by magnetic pole.

This dazzling māyā is such a terrible master  
Demanding her servant’s surrender cent percent  
Kicking him in the face, “try harder, work faster,”  
Although fully exhausted, no time to repent  
Only to learn that the ultimate prizes  
Are threefold miseries in ghastly form  
Too ill-fated to ever become wise  
As human being, dog, donkey or worm.

A slight attempt to get free on his part,  
Instantly paralyzed at the very start  
By the injection of lusty desire  
Degrading himself, becoming thief and liar,  
Only to enjoy that tiny pleasure  
Willingly takes distress beyond measure  
Then greedily tries to grasp his gain  
Which disappears, he attained only pain,  
As he is approaching his imminent death  
Perturbed and baffled, looking back in wrath  
māyā remains cold, he’s crying in vain.

Being a born loser, life after life,  
Still faithfully for contentment he’ll strive  
To be rewarded with grief and distress  
But this time, he thinks, he will meet success  
Blaming circumstance and situation  
But this time he’ll get his compensation;  
In this way he thinks he’s doing quite well  
Such is the potency of māyā’s spell.

This illusion became a fertile ground  
For Kali-yuga since five thousand years  
Expanding his kingdom, solid and sound,  
Dragging nations into wars very fierce  
From the very beginning continuously  
Drowning remnants of religiosity  
Like once flourishing cities, now forsaken  
Into the vast ocean of evil-makin’  
People misguided in all undertakin’  
Always disturbed, lazy, their habits unclean  
This age creates an intolerable scene.

Untouched, however, by all this tribulation  
Our paramprarā is running through the ages  
Above all material contamination,  
A chain of gold, pure devotees, perfect sages;  
Like the full moon you have gracefully risen  
And then spending a lifetime in preparation  
To fulfill your most beloved master’s mission,  
What you have done is beyond calculation.

Your appearance in this miserable place  
Is no doubt the most auspicious even,  
Compassion in person—-Your Divine Grace,  
For other’s welfare each minute you spent  
Preaching a truth far beyond time and space;  
To glorify you, for years we may intend  
To compose poems at speed of light’s pace  
And still there’s no way we’ll come to an end.

We’re wretched souls in hopeless condition  
From our eternal home gone so far astray,  
Somehow met with Lord Caitanya’s mission  
Somehow in human body crossing your way,  
That’s our only and genuine treasure  
Clearing the path to transcendental pleasure  
Far beyond the scope of a jñānī can trace  
At last on our way home through Your Divine Grace.

In this world wherever you went  
śāstra you taught, no need to invent,  
the way you did, extremely palatable  
exposing scientist’s view as abominable.

Wherever you set your lotus feet  
Touring the planet, your bhaktas to meet;  
Everywhere we have practically seen  
Any town and village to which you came  
The atmosphere turned nice and clean,  
The whole surrounding was not the same  
The environment revealed auspicious signs,  
Disarrangement wonderfully aligns  
In countries where the sun never shines,  
Never before the birds had sung so sweet  
Did they mean Your Divine Grace to greet?

Although the open wound of separation  
Leaves us with broken heart in lamentation,  
You left nothing unclear, what needs to be done,  
So please let our desires and yours become one;  
Please let his become our life and soul;  
Spreading Kṛṣṇa’s glory knows no substitute  
Only through you we will reach the goal  
And catch a glimpse of your preaching mood;  
On the flower paved road,  
Leading to Kṛṣṇa in His eternal and blissful abode.

*Your most humble servants at ISKCON*  
*Bangkok / Thailand,*

Ātmavidya dāsa / Gaura-kīcora dāsa / Jagad-ādija dāsa / Mahākratu dāsa / Nṛsiṁha Tīrtha dāsa and family / Sammohinī

## Bangladesh

*Nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

With great pleasure we again take the opportunity to write in praise of you for your VYĀSA PŪJĀ offering. Actually, we should be offering at every moment words in glorification of your activities, and there is no limit to such praises. But we feel that our words, however nicely composed, shall stand hollow without the real feeling that is meant to give them substance. O Lord Kṛṣṇa, at least this once, let our hearts full with sincerity so that we may properly praise your pure devotee. (Here stands a dilemma. Whatever we write is certain to be full of all the base qualities of which we are made, and even if externally it seems to be purely motivated, Śrīla Prabhupāda can see right through to all the rubbish desires in our hearts. Maybe it would be better to offer a few simple words and the rest mentally. But no, it is our duty to praise the spiritual master. A father accepts even a valueless gift from his small child because it is offered in a spirit of love. So, although we are unfit, and our words cannot increase Śrīla Prabhupāda’s greatness, still we must write.)

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, your presence is always keenly felt by us in everything that e do that you have given to us—-in your taped lectures and *kīrtanas,* in your books and instructions, in the morning program, in *prasādam,* in preaching, and now in *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛṭa.*

Therefore it is still a shock to us sometimes when we suddenly realize that you are no longer personally present with us. Like birds suddenly left to fend for themselves, we feel somewhat lost.

The Mayapur-Vṛndāvana festival will never be the same. No longer will your disciples anxiously await the next volume of your *Bhagavatam* purports. “Where is Prabhupada now? What wonderful activities has he now performed? What revelations has he just made?” The excited talk will never again pass through the corridors of your temples.

Still, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your great gift is to teach us that we are all eternal and are meant to serve the Supreme Eternal, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, through the grace of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. Therefore our remembrance of you is not some sentimental sob. There is no question of simply making some poetic offering to you once a year. You are the living (yes!) eternal Founder-*Ācārya* of ISKCON and will always remain the center of everything. Anyone who fails to recognize this will fall away. Real sincerity is shown by a sustained effort in following your instructions, especially in preaching.

Personally, when I consider my own position, I should be very unhappy. Practically speaking, you have given us eternal life, knowledge, and bliss in the service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa—but I am such a dullhead, I am still addicted to material, ghastly things.

The spiritual master’s business is to present complete knowledge of spiritual life to the disciple’s sincerity in purpose in attempting to please the *guru* set the rate of the disciple’s progress.

On VYĀSA PŪJĀ day, we are supposed to make an offering to the *ācārya,* but considering my own position, I am again begging from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Please force me to engage wholeheartedly in your service. I am ashamed to admit that without such pressure I will not do so—I am such a rascal, weak-hearted.

Actually, I need you to beat on my head till I submit—-but I know myself to be useless. I whimsically ask for required punishment, but when it comes, then I complain! Śrīla Prabhupāda, please have mercy on me. Certainly, my position is hopeless, but unless I am connected to you, though unfit, my existence is meaningless.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the offenseless chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa which you have given to us will ultimately bring one to enter the pastimes of Lord Caitanya and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Of course, I cannot aspire for such a position. Let me pray again and again that once in the coning crores of my rebirths I may at least once get your holy *darśana* and take your lotus foot dust as a decoration on my head. And if somehow or other I could become one of your intimate disciples and go universe to universe with you—that is the topmost desire of my heart.

*Your disciples and granddisciples in Bangladesh,*

PrabhaViṣṇu Swami / Ilāpati dāsa / Vyāpī dāsa / Rasika dāsa / Nisthūla dāsa / Nityānanda-prāṇa dāsa / Rādhānātha dāsa / Gaura-Gadādhara dāsa / Rāmacandra dāsa / Caitanya-caritāmṛta

## Baton Rouge

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. We were all in the most excruciating anguish, but you kindly come to our rescue. Now we are eternally indebted to you. The good fortune you have given is Kṛṣṇa Himself. We can never repay you for what you have done, but still we know one thing in this regard that is a very important instruction of yours. In the words of Your Divine Grace, “If you at all feel indebted to me, then you should preach vigorously like me.”

**The Instructions of the Guru**

Material existence is a blazing fire  
Proportionate to one’s material desire.  
We who are burning can understand  
That the pure devotee can give us a hand.  
It wasn’t until that you finally came  
That a soul could be found to put out the flame.  
Now we are given a golden chance  
You pull the strings and in that way we’ll dance.  
Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda we know that it’s true  
That you are the actual jagad-guru.  
And as your disciples and granddisciples too  
Now our pleasure in life is just to please you.  
We know your instructions, they are very complete  
No illicit sex, gambling, intoxication or meat.  
Also, sixteen rounds daily on our beads, at least,  
And, oh yes, on Sundays we should hold a big feast.  
As much as possible we should find a way  
To preach and distribute books every day.  
This is the main work in your divine mission  
This is the main work in your divine mission  
And it is being taken up by those who have listened.  
You said, “I’ve built the framework, now you fill it in.”  
Now there’s so much to do it’s hard to begin.  
But if we knock down this framework by falling for sin  
Then how will it be put back together again?  
Now what we have we must surely maintain  
And increasing is sure if from lust we refrain.

“Please distribute books!” is the order you gave  
And to that order we must make ourselves slaves.  
You have always been right, never going astray.  
So for spreading this movement, this is surely the way.  
Oceans of nectar and ślokas and meters.  
We want to flood the world with Bhagavad-gītās!  
And to add more to this literature ocean  
Rūpa Gosvāmī’s ocean of the Nectar of Devotion.  
And who could forget your colossal contribution  
Śrīmad-Bhagavatam for mass distribution.  
Once we remember in the wink of an eye,  
Caitanya-caritāmṛta without even a try.  
And the best magazine that has ever been read.  
The backbone of our movement, Back to Godhead.  
And so much more that our warehouses abound  
So there’s definitely more than enough to go ‘round.  
Your success was no secret, your formula true,  
Doing just what Bhaktisiddhānta wanted you to.  
So now it’s our turn, selling books we can’t dodge.  
We blindly follow our Guru Maharaja.

*Your servants in Baton Rouge,*

Raṅganātha dāsa / Yogīndra-vandana dāsa / Bhakta William / Bhakta Brent / Dr. Kumar / Dr. Seth / Premamayī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Janet / Mrs. Kumar / Mrs. Seth / Kapila Muni / Sarah / Raju / Madhuresh / Vinīta

## Benelux

I offer my respectful obeisances unto  
His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada,  
Who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
Having taken shelter at His lotus feet.

My obeisances are unto you,  
Servant of Sarasvati Gosvami,  
Who are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya  
To destroy impersonalism and voidism.

Our master, guide and savior,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
This homage unto you  
Is but a ghee lamp in the sun.

You have planted in us the seed of devotion,  
And we have seen in you what that love means.  
Unmotivated and uninterrupted service to Kṛṣṇa  
Has your life shown from beginning to end.

You have come from Vraja, the land of Govinda,  
To invite us all, back home to Godhead.  
It is your only concern how to save us fools.  
Let us never forget your divine instructions.

If this will be so, our life will be wasted,  
In miseries and pain, eternally lost.  
Sun is like Godhead, ignorance like darkness,  
And you are the sunshine, the pure devotee.

There is no secret to achieving the goal,  
But clearly we must learn your message.  
One should refrain from meat-eating and intoxicants,  
No illicit sex, speculation or gambling.

Live with devotees, sixteen round daily,  
Chant, dance, feast, and spread sankirtana.  
You have given us everything, for a little return  
And never kept anything for your own.

When you started you had nothing,  
No temples nor money, and you went alone from door to door.  
No helpers, nor servants, not even encouragement,  
But you always did whatever was needed.

If we still understand that you have come to save us,  
From the miseries and ignorance of our material existence,  
Struggling so hard to come to the West;  
Our everyday problems will seem only trivial.

But instead you have proved, with faith and conviction,  
That there is nothing higher than devotion to Kṛṣṇa.  
You have played the part of an ordinary human,  
Just to teach us, fallen, the way of success.

Everyone read in Prabhupada-lilamrita  
The wonderful deeds of your eternal pastimes.  
For us now it is easy, we simply have to follow  
And your divine footsteps will leas us along.

We have to be bold, simple and pure.  
And no compromising should enter our life.  
Let us all work for Kṛṣṇa as Prabhupada did  
And this will be our best VYĀSA PŪJĀ.

To change mlecchas and yavanas into Vaisnavas,  
This was the reason why Prabhupada came.  
The mission is now left for us to push on,  
We have to print books, make devotees, build temples.

In this way Prabhupada will smile on our effort  
And at his feet eternally, he will keep us with Kṛṣṇa,  
No one can deviate, no one can concoct,  
Without being united he will not be pleased.

Although we are incompetent,  
We will do better and better,  
And ISKCON will be the most powerful force  
And you Śrīla Prabhupāda, its eternal aśārya.

## Berkeley

U.C. has the Golden Bear  
You see that we have the Golden Avatāra.

Stanford U. researched the anti-material particle.  
Loving you reveals the anti-material person.

Sticks and stones may break my bones.  
But your words will never hurt us.

For $750.00 Air Singapore will fly you to India.  
By simply chanting your holy names monkeys can fly like Nārada Muni.

There are Māyāvādi “gurus” who are expert at taking conditioned soul’s Money.  
But you are expert at taking away our minds ( and leaving us breathless.)

The Guru does not come into this world to be a mundane wrangler over petty material things. He delivers the touchstone of the holy name. *Śrī hari-nāma* alone is truth. It is truth, it is truth, it is again and again truth. People may criticize him but all criticism is useless unless it is given by a devotee and is fully enlivening to devotional service. Please may all of our offenses go unnoticed. Our only desire is to just “become closer to you, heart-to-heart.” Personal meetings are not always necessary. You control from the heart. Didn’t you personally say that to me?

*Your fallen servants at New Jagannātha Purī Mandira*

## Bhaktivedanta Ashram (Sydney)

Your golden form indicates the dawn,  
Knowledge and renunciation by devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.  
Your penetrating pure words cleanse the heart of the conditioned souls  
So they can taste devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.  
Your transcendental books give us a look, at the form of the Absolute,  
Revealing devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.  
Your loving dealings have untold meanings that defeat the taste of nectar  
And we take to devotional service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.  
Your transcendental appearance is a chance to remember  
Your unlimited qualities.  
You are steady and clean, grave and equipoised.  
You are sane and honest, pure and perfect.  
You are gentle and straightforward, loving and kind.  
You change the life of the two-legged human gods, mlecchas and asuras,  
Giving in charity devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.  
You are the greatest mystic, perfect yogi, miracle-maker, servant of God,  
Our ever well-wisher.  
I offer my respectful obeisances unto you.

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

**Prabhupada-līlāmṛta**

Prabhupada appeared in splendor,  
To a sacred home he came,  
Tasting love of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa  
Trained to chant the holy name.

Encouraged by his saintly father,  
Ratha-yatra yearly he staged,  
Rousing all his friends and neighbors,  
Service to the Lord engaged.

Though at college, married, working,  
Kṛṣṇa he would ne’er forget,  
By the Lord’s divine arrangement  
Bhaktisiddhānta he then met.

“Preach in English, spread it wide,  
Lord Gauranga’s message bold,”  
Hearing well from saintly master  
His life’s mission did unfold.

Thus devoted to his guru  
Walking by his side one day,  
“If you ever get some money,  
Print my books,” he heard him say.

Selflessly he labored, writing,  
Printing books, his only aim,  
Sailed he then on Jaladuta,  
Ent’ring in Kali’s domain.

Spreading light amidst the darkness  
Sang he Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s name,  
Thousands joined his mission,  
Temples, schools and farms soon came.

Though he’s parted from our vision,  
In his books he can be found,  
Take the mercy, distribute them,  
Spread the holy name around.

*The residents of Bhaktivedanta Manor, London, England.*

## Bhaktivedanta Swami Guru-kula

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. We are your eternal servants, and may we always remember that we have no other business but to obediently fulfill your instructions. You wrote to us in Dallas in 1972, “Now I very much appreciate your activities for conducting our school to the highest standard of Kṛṣṇa consciousness behavior, and I consider your work the most important in the society because you are shaping the future generations of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness preachers, and this is not any small thing. So I am depending very much upon you all to assist Lord Caitanya in fulfilling His mission for saving the humankind from very quickly gliding into hell.”

In 1976, “Therefore the school should be moved to India, specifically to our new *guru-kula* project in Vṛndāvana , where the facility will be just to our needs of the *brahmacārī* to develop spiritually because it is based on authentic scripture. To live in Vṛndāvana is the highest perfection and to grow up in Vṛndāvana the greatest fortune.”

Again in Vṛndāvana in 1976 as you surveyed the *guru-kula* building going up, “There must be five hundred students and ten managers, that’s all. Let people be convinced.” Later on that year you said, “There is on scarcity of space. Vṛndāvana is a holy place. There is no government interferences so increase it—bring more students from all over the world. Then it will be unique, and you also make schemes to get Indian children from aristocratic families. Those who are born in high class rich families, and *brāhmaṇa* families, they are not ordinary.”

And then in your last days in 1977,”The *guru-kula* is meant for this purpose (preaching). Teach them and let them go round the world to preach….Let there be struggle for existence for that purpose.”

The purpose is clear. We must fulfill your orders. We must train a class of preachers, firmly fixed in the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, unshakably convinced, to deliver this message throughout the world. None of us understand how it is to be done, but we have *full* faith in your order, and we are the willing and loving instruments for your mission. Please deliver us from *māyā’s* temptation and allow us to become pure and have no desire separate from yours. Allow us to assist our *guru-*Godbrothers by training hundreds and thousands of their disciples as powerful preachers. Help us guide them from boyhood to manhood, making them strong preachers, burning with the missionary spirit of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement—-your ISKCON movement. Please allow us to become worthy, to fully surrender to you, to triumph over the darkness which sometimes clouds our minds, to cooperate, to teach the students to love Kṛṣṇa, to love Him ourselves.

We want your full satisfaction and mercy. Please remember us, and let us always remember you as you were in Vṛndāvana: encouraging the boys as they chanted and danced for you beneath the *tamāla* tree, taking pleasure in their *kīrtana,* smiling to hear them returning with *kīrtana* from the Yamunā, and drawing us all close to you like Grandfather Bhīṣma in your final days. We are remembering you in Vṛndāvana in your Bhaktivedanta Swami Guru-kula, and we remain,

*Your eternal servants,*

Dhanurdhara dāsa brahmacari / Rūpa-vilāsa dāsa adhikārī / Raghunātha dāsa brahmacari / Vyeṇkaṭa dāsa brahmacari / Nirāga devīdāsa brahmacari / Viśvaretāḥ dāsavanacari / Pūrṇacandra dāsa brahmacari / Candrikā-devī dāsī

## Bhaktivedanta Swami Guru-kula Student Offerings

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. On this day of your holy appearance, I will make an attempt to write something about your unlimited glories. Just as a father, being very affectionate towards his small child, appreciates even the broken language of his child, similarly, being most affectionate toward all your disciples, I hope you will accept this VYĀSA PŪJĀ offering.

You descended into this world for one purpose, which was to spread the message of love of God. An ordinary person cannot understand the nature of the appearance of a pure devotee. You were not obliged to appear in the world. But due to your kindness upon all fallen souls, you appeared to propagate God consciousness. You are like the full moon which dissipates the darkness of ignorance. Your activities while you were physically present were meant only to fulfill mission of your Guru Maharaja. You fully prepared yourself and came to the Western countries, and you didn’t compromise the teachings of the *Bhagavat-gītā.*

In the *Bhagavat-gītā* it is stated that the human form of life is meant for the cultivation of spiritual knowledge, and the executive heads of all states and planets are obliged to impart these lessons to the citizens by education, culture, and devotion. You have accomplished all three simply by writing your books, but also practically within our society. Therefore, you have made it possible for anyone to cultivate spiritual life.

You are decorated with all the qualities of a *sādhu.* Only a person who is pure can be humble. One may have many qualities and be capable of doing so many things, but until one is pure-hearted he can’t be humble. You said, “My only qualification is that I have heard nicely from my spiritual master.” This is the manifestation of your humility. Ordinarily, if anyone successfully performs an activity he becomes proud. You performed miracles but gave credit to your Guru Maharaja and Kṛṣṇa.

Anyone who is a pure devotee can influence the lives of many conditioned souls. This is confirmed in *Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya* 22.83):

*Kṛṣṇa-bhakti-janma-mūla haya ‘sādhu-saṅga’*  
*Kṛṣṇa-janme, tenho punaḥ mukhya aṅga*

**“The root cause of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa is association with advanced devotees. Even when one’s dormant love for Kṛṣṇa awakens, association with devotees is still most essential.”**

You were unlimitedly potent in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In just a short span of time you accomplished things that no one could accomplish in hundreds of years. Your name will be remembered in history for hundreds of years to come as the greatest *ācārya.* I can only repay my debt to you by always remaining indebted.

*Your disciple,*  
Dvārakādhīśa dāsa(age 18)

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. You are always thinking of how you can save all fallen conditioned souls from the material world which is full of misery, birth, death, old age, and disease. The pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa is full of compassion, soft-hearted like the lotus flower. You are *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,* you become unhappy seeing that others are suffering. You are more merciful than Kṛṣṇa because Kṛṣṇa fulfills all the desires of the living entities for sense enjoyment, but you have given us Kṛṣṇa, the reservoir of pleasure.

You went to the most degraded place in the world to distribute the nectarean message of Kṛṣṇa. You have solved the most dangerous fear of the living entity, which is repeated birth and death in this material world. That is the qualification of the absolute, bona fide spiritual master: he can stop birth and death and take us back home, back to Godhead. Therefore, you are my spiritual master. Yet I am prepared to take birth after birth for your service.

*cakhu-dan dilo jei, janme janme prabhu sei,*  
*divya-jnan hrde prokasito*  
*prema-bhakti jaha hoite, avidya vinasa jate,*  
*vede gay jahara carito*

**“He who has given me the gift of transcendental vision is my lord birth after birth. By his mercy, divine knowledge is revealed in the heart, bestowing** ***prema-bhakti*** **and destroying ignorance. The Vedic scriptures sing of his character.”**

You are always my lord birth after birth. Even though you have disappeared to join Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, still we have you in your books. You are the representative of Kṛṣṇa. You are the external manifestation of Kṛṣṇa. By serving you we serve Kṛṣṇa because Kṛṣṇa Himself has said, “One who claims to by My devotee is not so, but one who claims to be the devotee of My devotee is actually My devotee.”

Lord Siva also said to Parvati that there are different methods of worship, and out of all such methods the worship of Viṣṇu is the highest, but even higher than worship of Vishnu is the worship of the devotees of Kṛṣṇa.

I cannot practically describe your unlimited qualities and mercy. For me to describe you is like worshiping the sun with a little lamp. Whatever you have given to me, I can only offer that to you. Please bestow your mercy upon me. *Ohe vaisnava Thākura ami tomara kukkura.* O Vaisnava Thākura! I am your dog. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your eternal insignificant servant,*  
Prabhava dāsa brahmacari (age 18)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Having never come up to even the minimum standard of purity and conduct, I was never able to have very much of your exalted association. However, in Vṛndāvana now there are two *mūrtis* of you: You are so kind and fatherly, sitting on the *vyāsāsana,* with soft saffron clothes and gently folded hands; and, in scholarly eyeglasses, behind your desk, writing affectionate letters to your bona fide disciples.

Somehow, by your magnanimous mercy, you allow unlimited *darśana* and association even to me. In a million words, over a million lifetimes, I cannot thank you enough for letting me come to Vṛndāvana.

Please forgive my envious offenses and spiteful disregard of your instructions. Please allow me, by some small token, in some insignificant way, to serve the mission of your *saṅkīrtana* desire, and by taking the dust of the lotus feet of your real disciples upon my worthless head—please change me.

Viśvaretāḥ dāsa

## Bolivia

*om ajnana-timirandhasya jnananjana-salakaya*  
*caksur unmilitam yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namah*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

After hearing you for the first time, everything I had heard and everything I had experienced in my thirty years of age, I could see again with clarity. Now everything had meaning. Now I could see what was right and what was wrong. You were that light that immediately destroyed the ignorance of my life…my childhood, my boyhood, my youth, my years of study, my emotional experiences, sentimental experiences, artistic and philosophical experiences, etc., like one who sees with clarity a nightmare after waking up. Just then did I understand what is knowledge. Just then did I realize that what I was looking for in my whole life was knowledge.

The conference salon of Parco of the Prince of Rome was completely filled with people when I got there. One week before I had seen an advertisement for your conference with the name “Hare Kṛṣṇa Festival.” All those days were an anxious waiting that obliged me to meditate on that moment as something very important in my life. In the salon of that hotel they were projecting the film *Hare Kṛṣṇa People.* They cut it at the middle, turned the lights on, and I started looking around in all directions, sort of guessing what would be the key there. How can I describe that moment of your appearance? Spontaneously, I saluted you with both hands very much moved, and with an inconceivable reflex you returned my salute even though you were walking and looking ahead and I was no one side and standing inside the crowd. From that moment on I was completely absorbed watching you in every detail and listening, helping my ears with my hands. The whole atmosphere changed that moment, and all my worries vanished away. Every second my attraction was bigger and bigger for that “*guru* from India” whom I saw as so familiar in such a short time, not any more as a stranger.

I remember I got a little irritated to hear the very absurd questions people were asking and at the same time marveled at your infinite patience when you answered on a very simple level so everyone could understand. After that night everything changed for me. In the apartment in which I lived, I placed your photo. I remember I used to see it and feel attraction and fear at the same time.

How was I to imagine what was happening those days? Nonetheless, by your causeless mercy, which is the same as Kṛṣṇa’s, I was being saved from the *saṁsāra* of repeated birth and deaths.

Months before I had said, “O you, whoever you may be, please guide my life for I already have proved that every time I try to direct it is a failure and I go towards a more miserable life!” Now I could understand that my request was heard and that the Supreme Being put me in contact with the *jagad-guru* for whose association even the demigods try by all means to take birth on the same planet.

*Tad viddhi praṇipātena*  
*Paripraśnena sevayā*  
*Upakeṣñanti te jñānaṁ*  
*jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

Now, after seven years, by your infinite mercy, these words of Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavat-gītā* have come true as an irrevocable fact in my life. Now by your grace I can preach these things instead of barking like a dog or croaking like a frog. How can I respond to those incomparable gifts? It is useless to make calculations in this sense. For me you are as Kṛṣṇa Himself. No one can know Kṛṣṇa without knowing you. And no one will be able to forget you after knowing you.

Even if I were serving your lotus feet for millions and millions of lives, I will never be able to repay what you have given me with only one glance, with your salute, seven years ago. I do not deserve to be in your service, but I beg you to please not let me be entrapped by the illusory energy! I am only a dirty and miserable pig who does not want to give up the pleasures of the excrement and garbage of the material world and take the nectar of immortality and enjoy eternally your association and that of the Supreme Lord.

Please give me shelter in the shade of your lotus feet for without it I am completely helpless and without hope to get out of this entanglement of lust.

When I saw you in Vṛndāvana, I was feeling sure to be in the spiritual sky while you were there, but as soon as you went somewhere for preaching purposes, I could no longer understand Vṛndāvana without your presence. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, *māyā* is so strong that in your absence I used to feel so abandoned. What is going to happen with me now that you are gone forever? My affliction knows no bounds, for not then nor now do I serve you with sincerity. I am just a gross, lusty pig that survives in the well of material existence with only the hope of being able to serve you purely someday, not being dirty with the envy that surrounds me.

I want to get out of here, Śrīla Prabhupāda! I want to be always with you like five years ago. I remember at that time you told me to make Deities of Gaura-Nitāi.

Fortunately, after all I could make more Deities trying to please you. I had my workshop in the same building as the temple. And when you used to come to give *darśana,* I used to see you by the window; so I could offer you my obeisances without being seen, just like the women in Dvaraka when Lord Kṛṣṇa got to the city.

So, my emotion was very intense, but at the same time I could see that my capacity to experience the nectar of your association was very limited because of my contamination from my sinful activities in previous lives and in this one.

O master! O my father! When will I become free of false pride by being absorbed in your unconditional service? Especially now that you are gone, I feel completely like an orphan. I know that only one of your instructions can purify my heart if I put it in practice. And this is my highest aspiration: to be able to put in practice your instructions as they are. I can see that you have left the way back home perfectly marked. Give me the intelligence to follow it and so be able to teach this to others, for there are thousands and millions of confused living entities here that need your mercy. But I sure that now that you are so near Kṛṣṇa, you are closely directing this movement that you yourself said is Kṛṣṇa Himself.

On this day of your appearance, I humbly beg the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa the blessing of having enough intelligence to remain in this movement following your instructions and fighting against *māyā* in all moments of existence. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your useless servants of Bolivia Mandira*

## Boston

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace, A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvati Gosvami. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day, we are praying to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you will reveal a tiny drop of your unlimited glories so that we may properly recite them for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa and of all Vaisnavas. They are always eager to hear them. We know that you are not forced to respond to our prayer—especially one so devoid of devotion as this—but there is so much evidence of your unlimited mercy that even though we are such unqualified recipients, you will still respond.

What is the evidence? Anyone with a little intelligence will plainly see, if they will make the effort to examine your activities. But a through investigation has to begin with a little submission, something far too difficult for those who are conditioned by perpetual skepticism. We invite them to apply their logic and argument to *Śrīla Prabhupāda-liliamrta,* just as Śrīla KṛṣṇadāsaKaviraja Gosvami invites those interested in logic and argument to apply them to the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The result: they will find it strikingly wonderful. This is the real evidence. How could anyone challenge your determination to fulfill the order of your Guru Maharaja? Your purity of purpose overcame so many difficulties and hardships. It was so hard for you to begin the publication of *Back to Godhead,* but you were successful. It was so hard for you to get to America, but you were successful. It was so hard for you to convince the residents of the West that service to Kṛṣṇa was better than sensual fulfillment, but you were successful. And by your continuing potency, your success daily increases.

How can anyone challenge this? But still, if they do, we’ll deliver the evidence. We take this as our eternal duty to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: to tell everyone about your unlimited mercy. If anyone wants still more evidence, let them come and see for themselves your devotees continuing your mission. We, who were among the greatest skeptics, are becoming completely convinced of your mercy by being allowed to serve you and your most worshipful Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you millions of times, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for taking the time to make us see the futility of materialism and showing us the real purpose of life. Who knows how many births were in store for us had you not been so persistent to drag us back home. Now we are vowing to make this birth the last as we are anxious to join you wherever you are engaging in your Prabhupada-lila.

Begging to remain in the dust of your lotus feet.

*Your servants,*

**Boston ISKCON**

## Brazil

This age of Kali, a vast ocean of faults and obscurity, spread its influence, affecting every aspect of life. In the dense darkness of the age of quarrel and hypocrisy, there was practically no hope to see things as they are, no hope for light. There was only vain and ridiculous attempts of some stars in the from of science, political ideals, underground movements, false *gurus* and prophets, stars whose faint twinkling only managed to complicate the already obscured vision in the impenetrable darkness of Kali.

Taking advantage of this gigantic “cosmic blackout,” everyone, kings and subjects alike, concealed by this predominating, ominous darkness, violating the supreme law, the law of God. As the night grew darker and darker, everyone’s impious and abusive activities flourished. Everything was out of control, ignorance absolute.

But in the feast of horrors could not last longer. An enormous, brilliant full moon appeared on the eastern horizon. As it rose and moved westward, the dense darkness of ignorance was dissipated, and the suffering of the terrified masses was alleviated by is cooling rays. You are that full moon, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you and your trunks full of golden and silver nectar, the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,* putting an end to the fraudulence of Kali’s “insignificant stars.”

It is you who are carrying the brilliant message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, willing to penetrate every nook and corner in this dark material universe with your powerful rays of mercy and compassion, until you reach the core of the most impious soul’s heart.

*Nitāi-pada-kamala, koṭi-candra-suśītala,*  
*Je chāyāy jagata juṛāy*

Lord Nityananda’s lotus feet have the cooling effect of thousands of full moons; and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the personification of the mercy of Lord Nityananda, the original spiritual master. You are the living representative of those thousands of moons and their soothing rays. And it is this radiance of thousands of moons which are only you have brought to this point in history where Kali was enjoying without restriction, imposing his kingdom of ignorance on everyone. And now you have turned to Kali into a frightened fugitive, like a king who has been dethroned and exiled.

*Naṣta-prāyeṣv abhareṣu*  
*Nityaṁ bhāgavata-sevayā*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the personification of the *Bhagavatam,* and just as the *Bhagavatam* is eternal, so also are you. Your potency continually emanates, reaching beyond any imaginable limit, because there are no limits for transcendental sound; nor is there any obscurity which cannot be dissipated by the effulgent presence of the book *Bhagavata* or the person *bhagavata.* Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have brought to life the real meaning of the *Bhagavatam,* both in your instructions and by your personal example. You have made possible what appears to most impossible.

*Tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*Yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ‘nkitāni yac*  
*chṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*

The *Bhagavatam,* incarnated in your transcendental personality, has turned into a reality for this condemned age of Kali. There is no doubt that only by your mercy is it creating a revolution in the life of this impious and misguided civilization, a revolution based on the transcendental sound vibration from your lotus mouth. These transcendental vibrations are traveling to all parts of the world, melting the stone-cold hearts of the Iron Age’s victims.

Who could doubt that your appearance in this world and your sacred mission are anything other but the Supreme Lord’s plan to redeem this dreadful place? If anyone has some doubt, then we beg you again and again to give us sufficient intelligence and determination to deliver your message and your infinite mercy to everyone, everywhere; thus no one will lose the opportunity to take shelter at your lotus feet, the safest place in the three worlds.

But we cannot properly worship you by words alone; neither is a mere ceremony of fruits, flowers, and singing adequate. We would like to offer you all of Brazil, which is still insufficient, but at least we beg, following in your footsteps, to make this nation Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Thus in the near future “great” political leaders, religionists, scientists, and intellectuals can all participate in this ecstatic and most blessed event, your VYĀSA PŪJĀ offering.

*From all of the devotees from ISKCON Brazil, under the expert guidance of your faithful and beloved son, Hṛdayānanda Maharaja*

Portugese BBT / ISKCON Belem / ISKCON Belo Horizonta / ISKCON Brasilia / ISKCON Campinas / ISKCON Curitiba / ISKCON Fortaleza / ISKCON Nova Gokula / ISKCON Pindamonhagaba / ISKCON Porto Alegre / ISKCON Recife / ISKCON Rio de Janeiro / ISKCON Salvador / ISKCON Sao Paulo / ISKCON Vitoria

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It is not possible to describe your glories, just as it is not possible to describe the glories of Kṛṣṇa. We could praise you with millions of mouths for millions of years and still fail to adequately describe your wonderful qualities. However, it is our duty as your disciples and followers to attempt to offer you praise and in this way become humble.

For so long you struggled alone, Prabhupada, with only the order of Śrīla Bhaktivedanta Sarasvati Thākura as your shelter. It is inconceivable how anyone could remain so patient and determined through so many trying circumstances. It is also inconceivable how the world could have been so callous toward you for so long. We can understand only that Kṛṣṇa arranged these apparent trials in your life just to demonstrate the tolerance, humility, and compassion of a pure devotee and to enhance your personal glories.

We see in the lives of many great devotees how they had to endure much difficulty but were at last victorious simply by depending on Kṛṣṇa and following His order. Sometimes the eyes of even great stalwart ascetics like Bhīṣmadeva would fill with tears on seeing the plight of the Pāṇḍava princes and mother Kuntī, however, were assisted by so many great sages such as Vidura, Vyāsadeva, and others. The demigods would sometimes directly intervene in their favor, and Kṛṣṇa, Balarama, the great Yadu dynasty, and many other great devotees were personally present on the earth at the time.

Of course, it is natural that we your disciples and granddisciples will want to glorify you above all others, but we think that eventually everyone will come to see from the objective point of Vedic history that you are the greatest devotee and benefactor the world has ever seen. Transcendentally, you have surpassed the activities of even many of Kṛṣṇa’s incarnations. Of all Kṛṣṇa’s incarnations, Lord Caitanya is the most munificent because He is giving freely pure love of Godhead. Lord Caitanya came to inundate the whole universe with love of Godhead, but apparently He left without completing this mission. Of course, Lord Caitanya’s pastimes are always complete, but He wanted you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to take the credit for fulfilling His mission in this universe. You are His most intimate associate, and therefore no one can ever compare to you.

*Your servants at New Vraja-maṇḍala,*

## Brisbane

O Gurudeva, please give us protection at your lotus feet;  
The times we go on campus and they come out to hamper us  
And we can barely raise a song;  
When the Christians are swearing and the police are preparing  
To question us all night long.

O Gurudeva, please give us shelter at your lotus feet;  
When māyā becomes fragrant and the senses are awakened  
To the nectar which turns to poison;  
That unquenchable desire which burns like fire  
For which no amount of “nectar” can moisten.

O Gurudeva, please give us courage at your lotus feet;  
When we stand up to shout the facts right our  
And the cameras move in for more;  
When the crowds surge forward as they make that award  
And it is we who have the floor.

O Gurudeva, please give us patience at your lotus feet;  
When, preparing your city, palace, or sam’adhi  
With feeble and unsteady hand;  
When our plans come to fruitation in preaching your mission  
And Śrīla Bhaktivinoda comes down to this land.

O Gurudeva, please make us surrendered at your lotus feet;  
When the pots are to be done and we think it’s no longer fun  
In taking the surrendered stand;  
That we might consider without doubt when the halavā runs out  
That “this is certainly Kṛṣṇa’s hand.”

O Gurudeva, please make us enthusiastic at your lotus feet;  
That we may never take a “no” wherever the book may go  
And even give them a second or a third;  
To preach everywhere without considering it unfair  
To those who would have rather not heard.

O Gurudeva, please give us the remembrance of your lotus feet;  
So that victorious we may be and the whole world will see,  
That from beggars you have made so many kings;  
That for eons of time and in all places you may find  
Kṛṣṇa’s name, the most valued of all things.

*Your most insignificant disciples of Brisbane Yatra,*

## Cairo

*Dear beloved Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances, again and again. All glories to your soft lotus feet, which are my only shelter. In the summer of 1974, at the San Francisco International Airport, you so kindly gave me a glimpse of your true transcendental nature, and the sweet memory of those few moments of ecstasy remain embedded in my heart forever.

Devotees of all sizes, shapes and colors, from all corners of the planet, (and perhaps from even beyond), merged together in the ecstasy of your famous “airport arrival *līlā”,* completely oblivious to the unbelieving stares and wide-eyed wonders of the unsuspecting *karmīs.* Flowing robes and dangling beads decorated the long corridors, like the colored flags and festoons waiting to greet Lord Kṛṣṇa upon His triumphant arrival in Dvāraka. *Sanyāsīs, gṛhasthas,* mothers, *brahmacārīs,* and babies all united together in their love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, like a big, happy family reunion, with everyone present, save and except the kind and well-wishing father, whose eagerly anticipated arrival would make everything complete, and fully satisfying to everyone.

The dynamic intensity of spiritual emotion grew deeper and deeper with the arrival of more and more devotees. The atmosphere was electrified, cracking with excitement and suspense. “Prabhupada is coming! Prabhupada is coming!” Those prophetic words rang in our hearts. The *kīrtana* gained momentum, like a steam engine of a fast locomotive determined to reach its destination. “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare…..” Suddenly the *kītana* leader jumped into the air and broke the into a resounding, heart-throbbing chorus of our all-time favorite Kṛṣṇa conscious melody—-*Jaya* Prabhupada, *jaya* Prabhupada, *jaya* Prabhupada, *jaya* Prabhupada—clearly indicating to the waiting crowd that the moment everyone had been waiting for had finally arrived.

I pushed and squeezed my way thorough the pulsating mass of devotees, desperately trying my utmost to catch even a fleeting glimpse of your transcendental from, as you walked from the customs checkpoint through the arrival gate. Peering intensely at each successive, emerging body, and the pinpoint of concentration of a *yogi (*for once in my life) I was struck with horror and disappointment to hear a faint voice struggle to announce above the swelling mel’ee: “This is the wrong door. Prabhupada is coming from over there.” Confusion! Panic! A wave of bodies, no less powerful than the rolling tide of the Pacific Ocean, surged forward in perfect unison, and without my feet even touching the ground, I arrived at the next gate, pinned against the metal railing separating the newly arriving passengers from their loved ones. This was the chance of a million lifetimes, and everyone knew it. There was no time to be shy.

The deep longing of the crowd to see you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was nothing less than the deep longing of the spirit soul to get free from the clutches of *māyā* and at long last attain the soothing shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s loving lotus feet. You were the living, approachable, and all-merciful connection with Kṛṣṇa. And your arrival that night signified the perfection of our long, difficult sojourn through this hellish material world of repeated birth and death. The event was spiritually momentous, and also contagious, as even a number of airport personnel dropped their duties, abandoned their posts, and hastily ran to join the devotees in their glorification of the all-attractive, irresistible, *jagad-guru,* spiritual master of the universe.

Suddenly, most gloriously, like the appearance of the rising morning sun on the horizon, your beautiful lotus face, decorated with your unforgettable, transcendental smile illuminated the entire airport.

Cries of ecstasy and devotion filled the air. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! *Hari bol!* All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda! Thousands of hurried obeisances were performed in a frantic effort not to miss even a second of your most wonderful association. In the mad rush that ensued, one devotee’s watch was kicked completely from his wrist and sent spinning in the opposite direction, but (amazingly) he didn’t care. He just wanted to look at you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your presence was so pleasing and beautiful, that nothing else had any importance.

Calmly, smoothly, like a royal, white swan floating on a lotus-filled lake of cool, crystal-clear waters, you glided gracefully through the heaving crowd, far removed from the chaos and euphoria which surrounds you, fully conscious of Kṛṣṇa, your beloved Lord, at every second.

One boy gave you a large piece of fruit of some kind, and with familiar cane in hand, you walked through the aisle, silently pondering his gift with a dignified air of genuine interest and amusement, revealing to us in full view, right in the most unexpected place, the most profound, yet, reassuringly, simple personality in the whole universe.

As this most amazing, mystical procession continued along its way, I was fortunate enough, by Kṛṣṇa’s grace, to get right beside you. And by your causeless mercy, I received a tiny, little insight into your true identity.

I began to call your name, again and again: “Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda…” You so kindly responded. Like a tremendous flash of lightening blazing the heavens, your deep, lotus eyes, twinkling with the ointment of pure love and devotion spoke to my soul. “I am your eternal father, your eternal spiritual master.”

Instantaneously the whole airport was bathed in brilliant rays of dazzling golden light, like pure spiritual sunshine coming down from the kingdom of God. The bustling crowds, the terminals, the luggage trolleys, the periodic flight announcements over the P.A. system, the countless advertisements peppering the walls, everything, just faded away, and there was nothing left but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the shelter of my soul, standing before me in glorious, resplendent light.

In a quick, perfect motion you entered your waiting car, smiled softly, and with folded palms, as a last kind gesture of your love and appreciation, disappeared into the late San Francisco night ( a beautiful swan taking flight) leaving your disciples and admirers standing completely stunned and awestruck in the pavement behind you.

The long ride from the airport to the old Valencia Street temple on board the rented bus, specifically acquired for the historic occasion, was no ordinary ride.

Gazing incredulously at the continuous stream of endless cars and trucks flowing across the monstrous freeways and massive bridges of America’s great “city of lights,” I could actually perceive, for the first time in my life ( and perhaps the last), the unmistakable, undeniable presence of Paramātmā in the heart of every living entity. Every fingertip motion of every driver, of every car, of every lane, of every highway, of every state, of every country, of every planet situated in the stem of the lotus flower springing from the navel of Garbhodakaśāuī Viṣṇu was minutely controlled and directed by the vast, perfect intelligence of Supersoul. It was so obvious! I marveled at my sheer stupidity for not having realized the simple of truths, which you had been telling us over and over again, day after day, for so many years. “Kṛṣṇa is in everyone’s heart! Kṛṣṇa is in every atom! He’s everywhere! He’s conscious….He’s a person! Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead!!!!! It was incredible.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your heart is a great big ocean of transcendental mercy, filled with the most valuable, diamondlike teardrops of your unalloyed love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Miraculously, those rare, spiritual teardrops are showering like rainfall upon the dry, parched sands of this desertlike material world, nourishing the tender roots of innumerable devotional creepers, planted by your loving glance, and destined by your desire, to grow and blossom into lush, green gardens of pure devotional service, filled with the most delicious fruits and fragrant flowers of *bhakti-rasa.*

By your unlimited kindness, one such teardrop has fallen upon my head, and although I am so fallen and wretched, being severely infected with the qualities of low-grade men completely devoid of spiritual life, still, that precious teardrop has given me great hope.

Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*Begging to become your perfect servants, birth after birth,*

Padmapāṇi dāsa / Tribhuvanātha dāsa / Bhakta Ahmed / assemble devotees

## Calcutta

*Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

The sun is appreciated, glorified, and renowned by all because of its rays of sunshine, which carry its energy to the earth for the benefit of all its inhabitants. Similarly, Kṛṣṇa is solely appreciated through and glorified by the medium of a pure devotee like Your Divine Grace, for it is through your effulgent personality that Kṛṣṇa manifests His grace and benedicts us. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the comprehensive proof of Kṛṣṇa’s love for us, the proof that He really cares for us fallen, conditioned souls, for would He have sent in our midst one like you were it untrue?

Your magnanimity is all-pervasive. The sunshine may not care to enter into dark dingy places such as caves or places or people that choose to barricade themselves from its light; but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have penetrated even the most dismal wretched places—like our hearts—-so densely enveloped by the fortifications of false ego that we have erected around ourselves.

Your illuminating appearance in the midst of us wayward and lost souls has pierced the dense gloom of ignorance that has for so long shrouded our senseless existence. Now, for once, we see that there is a purpose, there is a goal, and there is a way, a way flooded by the luminous light of your loving guidance, your eternal instructions.

You imparted to us, in works that transcend time, the eternal truth of the *Bhagavatam—*the knowledge that is radiant as the sun. And just as the sunshine evaporates the ocean’s waters, or water concentrated in one place, and converts it into rain which is then dispersed to nourish the driest regions, so you have absorbed the ambrosial contents of *sanātana-dharma* from Bhārata-varṣa and distributed it all over the world. Thus you have slaked the thirst of us whose lives were parched by the frustrating quest for some happiness in this desertlike material world.

You installed in our decrepit and putrid lives a purity and freshness. The dank musty airs of illusion were dispelled in the same way that the sunshine would make even the most gloomy of places fresh by its appearance. Again, as the rising sun( by casting its rays) heralds a new day, your appearance, too, has heralded a new era in which the chanting of the holy name has spread all over the world. You have roused us from our forgetful slumber and declared Godhead is light, nescience is darkness.

You have dazzled us by your magnificent and brilliant personality. Your life is a shining example before us, so much so that in all its intensity it cannot be understood by us, for our vision is limited, weak, and conditioned. Even though we can appreciate only so very minutely the fullness and extent of your shoreless glory, nevertheless this much we know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you are the light of our lives, our beacon of hope, our all in all.

We pray in earnest to you, who are so kindly permitting us to serve you in the place of your appearance, which always helps us to remember our eternal debt to you—a debt that can never be repaid but only increases as we try to repay it—because all we can do, all that we are made of, is only your mercy.

Please kindly bless us so that we can always remember your glorious pastimes in Śrīdhāma Calcutta.

*Your eternal sevants,*
