## New Delhi

*Gurudeva!*  
*kṛpā-bindu diyā, koro’ ei dāse,*  
*trṇāpekhā ati hīna*  
*sakala sahane, bala diyā koro’,*  
*nija-māne spṛhā-hīna*

**Gurudeva, by a drop of mercy, make this servant of yours humbler than a blade of grass. Giving me strength to bear all trials and troubles, make me devoid of all desires for personal honor.**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Hastināpura (Delhi) is where many of your initial inspiration came. In this city you struggled to sell your *Back to Godhead* magazine and printed your first books, the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam.* The same shops and markets from which you asked help to go to America and preach *bhāgavata-dharma,* we also go making life members, distributing your books and chanting *hari-nāma.* Many people we see say to us, “Oh, I knew Swamiji. We could have never known he would do what he did.” Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s friends and relatives didn’t know of Hid divine origin, your pastimes here have similarly unfolded.

Hastināpura is a place where you manifested your opulences of tolerance, fortitude, great faith, and, of course, patience. Whenever the heat becomes intolerable, the dust overcoming, or the people not receptive, the devotees here relish remembering the great strength of purpose you exhibited in the very same circumstances. The example in your biography is given that your fervent desire to distribute *Back to Godhead* did not wane even in the 100-degree heat, and consequent heatstroke. Nor did your enthusiasm diminish when the numberless politicians and *krorapatis* refused to help you. Because of your example there is no turning back from the preaching endeavor. You have given the formula by which the whole world can become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

We find it difficult to understand the extent to which Lord Kṛṣṇa has manifested His mercy in you. Looking to the past where ignorance was preeminent we cannot fathom the change you have accomplished in our lives. From various levels of ignorance, like small insects without a clue where to go, what to do, you have changed animallike individuals into devotees. It’s unfortunate that the dense layers of *ahaṅkāra* have not left our consciousness—-for descriptive words from persons not advanced only shadow your illustrious character. Through the agency of your mercy so many living entities are now and in the future able to be freed from the cycle of birth and death to attain to the *paraṁ dhāma,* their original home.

Not within the expanse of the entire Vedic history on record has there ever been a personality as magnificent as yourself. The Vedic culture, reaching in *nadir* in the middle of this century, diminished to a faint glow, almost indistinguishable. From the core of that ailing body came a personality so dynamic, and wonderfully compassionate, that again the pure teachings of the Vedic texts are being presented in every part of the world. In only fifteen years one person out of every forty has received transcendental literature about pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your glories will continue to expand unlimitedly over the next ten thousand years. You will be looked upon as the sole devotee responsible for delivering Lord Caitanya’s mercy to billions of living entities. Every individual will be inspired by your great love for the Lord and thus be saved from repeated birth and death. Only one of the greatest and most intimate devotees of the Lord could accomplish such an astonishing feat. It is it you that this credit will go; Lord Caitanya has made it so. We can only wonder at our great fortune to have seen you walk in our midst. Please grant us just one drop of your mercy to enable us to praise you commensurate with your exalted self.

## New Jersey

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will the day come when the entire world will understand and praise your glories? Caitanya Mahāprabhu had predicted that the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa would be heard in every town and village, and you Śrīla Prabhupāda, are proving yourself to be the topmost devotee and most dear associate of the Lord by exhibiting unflinching determination to fulfill His desire, and actually making it a tangible reality. Thus millions of people are receiving the mercy of Mahāprabhu in the form of the holy name, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā, Kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and the entire ISKCON movement, which is nondifferent from you. This is *your* unlimited mercy.

*Guru Kṛṣṇa-rūpa haña śāstrera pramāṇe: “*According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa.” O Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will the day come when we willfully understand and properly praise your unlimited glories?

*sādhu-saṅga-kṛpā kimvā kṛṣṇera kṛpāya*  
*kāmādi ‘duḥsaṅga chādi śuddha-bhakti pāya*

**“One is elevated to the platform of devotional service by the mercy of a Vaisnava, the bona fide spiritual master, and by the special mercy of Kṛṣṇa. On that platform, one gives up all material desires and the association of unwanted people. Thus one is elevated to the platform of pure devotional service.”**

Please bestow your mercy upon us so that our service may become pure and unflinching, as is yours, and we may be able to properly glorify and assist you in the spreading of this great movement. Although we are entirely undeserving, please keep us eternally engaged in the service of your lotus feet. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your menial servants at New Jersey ISKCON,*

## New Orleans

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our obeisances. Your mercy is immeasurable, and we pray for the means to acquire it. Striving for sensual pleasures, we had become lost in an ocean of nescience. Then, exactly as Nārada Muni saved Mṛgāri, the hunter, you rescued us from the darkest regions of ignorance. Our lives had no value; we were aimlessly directing ourselves to hell. Then, without notice your mercy appeared.

The eternal form of Kṛṣṇa, His holy name, His service, His *prasādam,* His pure devotee—your divine self, your *darśanas,* your instructions, so many spiritual gifts you presented us. More than we could conceive, and certainly more than we deserved, but causelessly you gave them to us. No one had ever given so much and asked for so little in return. These gifts were not ordinary, not mundane or temporary, but gifts meant to awaken our spiritual relationship with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This was real mercy. And before we could show our appreciation, you were gone.

And, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it would be material to consider you gone. You are your gifts, and they live with us in service eternally. Through service you will always be appreciated, worshipped, and loved. We beg to remain in your service.

*Your servants in New Orleans*

## New Varsana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We take this opportunity on this most blessed day, the appearance of Your Divine Grace, to rekindle in our hearts your intense mood for spreading the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We are all characterized as men who have but short lives, who are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky, and above all, always disturbed. We are born into this world with no good qualities, and our mentality is honeycombed with the most offensive thoughts. How is it then, that we find ourselves engaged in serving you? It is only your unbelievable compassionate mercy. You have made dancing dogs into pure Vaisnavas. This feat is unparalleled in the history of the world, and the historians of the future will note that it was you who came alone to the West to deliver this most profound knowledge.

How can we possibly repay this immense debt which we owe you? The debt is too astronomical in proportion for our tiny, useless brains to comprehend, but if we sincerely follow your every instruction, then perhaps we will be able to please you. For the disciple to satisfy the spiritual master is the essence of one’s life. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our very life air. Your divine presence and instructions are pumping life into us all.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your patience and protection. Please don’t kick us away, for your mercy is all we are made of.

*Your unworthy servants at New Varsana Farm,*

## New Vrindaban

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“*He lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with Him*.”

Your Divine instructions are eternally with us. We are unable to sever ourselves of them any more than water its liquidity or salt its salinity. You called it our “occupational duty,” unshakable and inviolate, to worship and serve Lord Kṛṣṇa with love and joy.

This is *bhakti,* devotion. The eternality of the spirit soul established in devotion you taught us, following faithfully, working and serving in eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord with form and personality. What did we know of all this before you?

Love, you emphasized, *bhakti.* God is conquered by love, by *bhakti,* and by no other means. That has always been at the core. And all that you did, you did out of pure love for Kṛṣṇa. You served as paradigm for us to follow, example of pure love in action. Not sitting in meditation on some void, but acting out of love for the Supreme Person—that is your life and your message.

August 17, 1968, our first year here at New Vrindaban, you wrote us: “Today they will observe VYĀSA PŪJĀ ceremony (my birthday anniversary), so from this day I will be stepping onto the seventy-third year. I hope the remaining days of my life may be utilized to serve all you Western devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Please pray to Kṛṣṇa that He may give me the necessary strength to discharge the duty entrusted by my spiritual master. ”

As your prayer was to discharge the instructions of your spiritual master, we now look to your instructions. “Now we can work with great enthusiasm for constructing a New Vrindaban in the U.S.A New Vrindaban is nothing physical or bodily, but purely spiritual and for the glorification of the Lord, Śrī Hari. If we actually keep this view in front, certainly we shall be successful in our progressive march.”

Now you, our ever well-wisher, more than ever with us, are verily leading this march of spirit souls back to Godhead. Cast by *karma* on this middle planet, enjoying and suffering in our heavens and hells, at unknown junctions in the universe, born into ignorance and darkness, receiving parents and bodies, nourished by sensory knowledge, how can we know which way is up? Until you came, we sat puzzled in the darkness of our Socratic cave, bedazzled by flickering shadows, not knowing which way to go, or even whether to go at all. Then you came with your matchless gift—free passage to Kṛṣṇaloka. *Where?* “Kṛṣṇaloka,” you said, “the highest planet of the innumerable spiritual planets in the *brahma-jyotir,* planets far, far greater in number than all of the planets in the material world. There are millions and billions of material universes with trillions of planets and suns, stars and moons, but this whole material creation is only a fragment of the total creation.”

And, out of grace and compassion, you presented us with easy journey to other planets: and, the warning: “Not all roads lead to Goloka Vṛndāvana.” While so-called *gurus* told their students to take any road, you pointed to Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s timeless words: *devān deva-yajo yānti mad-bhakā yānti mām api.* “Those who worship the demigods go to the planets of the demigods, but My devotees ultimately reach My supreme planet.” (Bg. 7.23)

Now, seated in your Palace of Gold and in far-flung *vyāsāsanas* in Europe, Asia, Africa, Australia, the Americas—-now there are Kṛṣṇa temples *everywhere!* You have multiplied yourself, as an original candle multiplies its light, to shed the light of knowledge to millions. Yes, millions. When a spiritual body enters the material pond, it is untouched, like the *paramahaṁsa,* but it sends forth countless spiritually repercussive ripples without end. How can we begin to approximate the extent of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement? What was the extent of Christianity just five years after the death of Christ? Was St.Peter opening centers in Auckland and Pindamonhangaba? Murwillumbah and Tehran? Mauritius and Mombasa? Yes, you have so quickly multiplied yourself. The sun never sets on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your *vyāsāsana* is always shedding light to guide us day and night.

Now your words are inundating the planet with transcendental knowledge of the Supreme Person and His abode. Books everywhere in all languages! Your divine books! Who could have presented them but you? Who could have ever explained the pastimes of the Lord Kṛṣṇa but you? Many have tried, and all failed. *Bhagavat-gitā* has been with us for five thousand years, but the glory of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s words awaited enunciation by your lotus lips. Squinting in our cave, we couldn’t understand until you came with the Vedic torchlight, lighting up our world with the *mahā-mantra* and showing us that “unto Me” means unto the person Kṛṣṇa.

“Always think of Me and become My devotee. Worship Me and offer your homage unto Me. Thus you will come to Me without fail. I promise you this because you are My very dear friend.”

Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could have never guessed what this means. Your life itself serves as example of *how* it’s to be done. A patient father, you smilingly watched our first stumbling steps. Now, dear father, *jagad-guru,* seated in your Golden Palace and in your temples around the world, smilingly witness and prod us on our progressive march back to Him. Grant us the grace to follow and serve you with greater determination. Inspire us to reject whatever does not advance Kṛṣṇa consciousness and accept whatever does. Help us burn all the garbage of past *karma* and activate our desire to attain Him through service to you. Kindly, out of your infinite mercy, forgive our offenses and release our material bonds that we may realize your service in the purity of *bhakti.*

*Inmates of New Vrindaban*

## New York City

*Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

All glories ! All glories! All glories unto you. You are Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s most perfect and dear confidential servitor. O glorious messenger, you are the pure transparent medium, the *parama-guru,* the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the sum total of all the demigods, and the manifest representative of Supersoul. You are the crest jewel amongst Vaisnavas. Your lotus feet are the shelter for all mankind. No one can estimate your potencies as you are fully empowered by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to execute His mission. You are unlimited mercy itself.

We are all lost. We struggle futilely on a rudderless ship in stormy waters. We are doomed without your intervention against opposing elements. Subtle contamination ever seeks to work its destruction. The shroud of forgetfulness of your instructions can always cover us. Please play your drum and lead the *kīrtana.* Please do not allow us to deviate from your path. Your sons and grandsons, daughters and grand-daughters, all serve you in separation. O spiritual grandsire, your family awaits your command.

We unworthy beggars pray at your lotus feet that you bestow your mercy on us. Please allow us to serve you eternally. Without your satisfaction we are failures. We are very insignificant and wretched, and are bereft of any value whatsoever unless we follow your order. You are our lord and master, captain and guide, and eternal father. We your fledgling brood beg you to protect us. May we somehow or other please you. That will be our ultimate perfection.

## Ottawa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. *Jagad-guru!* Spiritual master of all the world! You are the mercy incarnation of Śrī Hari and the only shelter of your faithful followers, the most fortunate recipients of your causeless mercy. How can we conceive, in our present state of blindness, the untold dedication and compassion that moved you to come from Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhama into the raging storm of Kali-yuga to pull us out of the gutter of senseless materialism and hedonism and bring us with you within the shelter of the house of *bhakti,* your society and life mission, thus changing our miserable destiny from self-annihilation to self-realization and offering us, your most unworthy children, the most precious and uplifting association of your pure words and your pure followers. This miracle, though so often enacted on such a worldwide scale, is still ignored by the masses of people today. Let us make our life’s work a relentless attempt to bring your glories into everyone’s home, within everyone’s heart. Thus, by witnessing your life’s work of inconceivable dedication, the unfortunate humanity will awaken from the darkness of ignorance to the bright sunlight of your soothing words and effulgent character, emblems of the pure soul’s eternal loving exchange with the reservoir of all pleasure, the embodiment of all perfections, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, your most intimate friend and protector.

As your disciples, we have been left with an immense legacy: to follow in your footsteps and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness on a massive worldwide scale, to redirect humanity from its present course of self-destruction towards a God conscious world family working together under the benevolent protection of the supreme father. By forgetting the supreme benefactor, only quarrel and misery have spread far and wide; the symptoms of our disease are ever increasing, but the remedy is one: learn from the enlightened pure lover of God the course back to sanity and freedom from all miseries.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that by studying your spotless character and life’s work, specifically related in your sublime biography and all of your books, we will gain ever-fresh inspiration and strength to follow your pure example and work for the salvation of upliftment of all mankind. Thus we will be allowed to be your pure representatives and attempt to give every single man on this planet the opportunity to bathe his heavy and weary heart in the life-giving elixir of your potent words and effulgent smile, which alone can enliven the soul’s dormant, pure desire for the eternal service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

*We beg to remain eternally, your most unworthy servants of New Kulinagram,*  
*ISKCON Ottawa, Canada,*

Lakśmīnātha dāsa brahmacari / Gaura dāsa brahmacari / Gokulānanda dāsa adhikārī / Līlāśuka-devī-dasi, Gopeśa / Ghośa Thākura dāsa adhikārī, Giriraja-devī-dasi, Jaya Śacīnandana / Nātha dāsa adhikārī, Līlā-devī dāsī / Sarvamayī-devī-dasi, Nitya-lila / Bhakta Denis / Śaṅkara dāsa adhikārī, Śampati-devī dāsī / Nārada Munī / Nīla Mādhava dāsa adhikārī, Deva-devī-dasi, Arjuna

## Panama

As a pure devotee of the Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda exhibited unlimited, transcendental qualities  
Not only engaging himself in perfect devotional service  
But in giving us, the fallen souls, so many opportunities  
To taste our original and sweet spiritual bliss.

His wonderful mission captured the attention of the great souls  
And life’s perfection and ultimate goal is easily reached  
Only by surrendering and engaging in service as Prabhupada’s dolls.

It is very difficult to understand his loving mood,  
But if we want to cross this ocean of miseries we should;  
What Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us is invaluable,  
And great souls shall try to appreciate it by following his footsteps.

Preaching the transcendental message of Śrīmad-Bhagavatam  
And Bhagavad-gītā  
As well as chanting the Lord’s holy names like Kṛṣṇa and Rama,  
His spiritual body revealed total devotion,  
And his grave face was the sign of unlimited erudition.

On this auspicious day of his appearance,  
Let us beg to Lord Kṛṣṇa that we always may be able  
To remember the transcendental activities of him  
The best among mahā-bhāgavatas.

And try to serve him unconditionally day after day  
Through the lotus feet of his divine representatives  
(such as Hṛdayānanda Maharaja), so in this way we  
can taste the nectar of a perpetual Vyāsa-pūjā.

*Your fallen servants of Panama Mandira,*

Akhilapati dāsa brahmacari / Śaunaka dāsa brahmacari / Māyeśa dāsa brahmacari / Parikṣit dāsa brahmacari / Ṛksarāja dāsa brahmacari / Rūpa Gosvami dāsa brahmacari / Bhakta Sabino

## Perth

Many years ago in a room colored blue  
A servant of Sarasvatī, faithful and true  
Did make his appearance, by the Lord’s sweet will  
The prophecy of Bhaktivinoda to fulfill.

In youth by his father this ṭhākura was taught  
To worship Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and pull Jagannātha’s cart,  
And in Calcutta station he would eagerly scan  
Timetables to dhāmas, Purī and Vṛndāvana.

Then at age twenty-six by the transcendent scheme  
Gurudeva he did meet and his orders receive:  
To preach the Lord’s glories from east side to west,  
Kṛṣṇa-bhakti from birth, his mission now blessed.

Then when most men are dying, at three score and ten,  
Alone but for Kṛṣṇa’s Bhāgavatam, by his pen,  
He broke Hindu custom and to New York he came,  
In Tompkins Square Park chanting Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s name.

Thus ensued revolution in the hearts of the world,  
The sincere came to hear his words, forceful and bold;  
“Bhagavān is your father, Kṛṣṇa-dāsa is your name,  
and pure prema-bhakti your rightful heirs’ claim.  
“Surrender to Kṛṣṇa! Preach His name’ cross the globe,  
endear yourself to Him and reach His abode.”

Since the birth of Lord Brahmā, nobody did see  
More potent a preacher, a pure devotee;  
Even Kṛṣṇa-Caitanya thru Bhārata-varṣa did go  
But Śrīla Prabhupāda thru his books, the world knows.

Historians, teachers, scientists, priests  
Welfare workers, professors, he called them all cheats!  
“You rascals, there is a soul, minute but great;  
add this one to your thoughts before it’s too late!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s greatness the world doesn’t know  
But to this end the sankirtana army does go  
To every town and village ‘til everyone sees  
Prabhupada’s matchless gifts, fruits of Caitanya’s tree.

Your instructions, your love, your mercy I crave  
And by Caitanya’s order, fallen souls we will save;  
O spiritual master, your divine lotus feet  
Taught the Jagāis and Mādhāis to dance in the street.

You taught by your words, your thoughts, and your deeds  
O Narasiṁha-guru, destroying the seeds  
Of thousands of sins of souls meant to die  
You turned sinners to saviors and made stonehearts cry.

Your tireless work and devotion to God,  
Turned matter to spirit, crippled minds into broad.  
Describing your glories, Ananta could not do,  
Please give me the power to recollect you.

With straw in my mouth I fall down on the ground  
And bathe in the dust of your mercy profound  
O epitome of bhakti, Kṛṣṇa’s will is your mace,  
Please force me to take shelter of Your Divine Grace.

*My dear beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

What shall I write to you on this, the most auspicious of all occasions? Of course, the devotees will all espouse your unchallenged, transcendental glories, which are lauded even by the demigods in heaven. But on this occasion I can think of nothing but my relationship with you. After all, you are our father, having given us real birth, unlike that of the cats and dogs. You are our physician, giving the remedy to that great material disease that has overcome us all; and you are our life and soul, for without remembrance of your lotus self even the holy *dhāmas* seem like ordinary places.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can you do this to me and others? Like Kṛṣṇa, you came and exhibited your beautiful smiling face and after capturing all our hearts you too quickly disappeared from our sight. Of course, you, just as Kṛṣṇa, remain in the heart of your lovingly devoted disciple. But I am not satisfied with that, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for I have always been a greedy person. I want your form as well as your instructions.

You once told me that I was understanding this philosophy of Kṛṣṇa nicely and to read your books more and more and thus be enthused to distribute them. Years later I am still falling short of pleasing you, but I know you can see me struggling with the *maya,* and somehow you must be pleased that I am still here. That also is only by your grace.

Someone told me that you once said that the spiritual master knows all of his disciples intimately and that you personally remembered each and every one of them. I recall when you came to Melbourne in 1975. Each night you were giving lectures on the *Bhagavad-gītā.* I had to do traveling *sankirtana,* and so some nights I would miss out on your *darśana.* Then one night after your lecture, I returned late and came alone to give you my feelings in a letter and some *dakṣiṇā.* After prostrating myself before you, you said something that I did not catch. Again you said, “You were not in the *kirtana?* I explained that I had been out distributing your books and we discussed a little which books sold best. But it was not till later that I realized you had been aware of my absence. Loving father, once you came to me in a dream. I had the realization that you were greater than Jesus, preaching worldwide and delivering the fallen.

Causelessly (and undeserving ) I got to live with you in the same house for ten days (that was here in Perth!) personally serving you and doing *sankirtana* in the spiritual master’s presence. That was my perfection, to be your humble, menial servant and come to present my daily begged alms at your feet and to take your remnants.

This is all I want, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This worldly life of home, children, wife—I know it all simply separates me from you by time. As soon as I become serious to execute your orders, that will hasten me to you. I know within that I have a loving relationship with you as a father loves his son and a son loves his father. Whoever cares for the temporary designations of father and mother in this world is destined to suffer; they are here one day and gone the next.

However, you are like Kṛṣṇa, and you have disappeared from our vision just to increase our love for you. And when we become mad with desire to see you your lotus feet, when we will run everywhere in any capacity to serve you, then you will again appear to us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please pray to Kṛṣṇa to hasten that day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know that somewhere at your feet there is an atomic position where I can remain. I won’t be much bother if you will just allow me to stay there forever.

*Your servants and sons of Perth Yatra,*

Upananda dāsa / Puruṣottama dāsa / Gaṇeśa dāsa / Ambikā-devī dāsī / Śubhalakṣmī-devī dāsī / Narottamī

## Philadelphia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

This universe of measured duration is glorified by the numerous appearances of the Lord and His pure devotees. Our Vaisnava calendar records so many auspicious and holy days, but your appearance in this world is the most blessed event, the most auspicious of all holy days. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have descended from Goloka Vṛndāvana, bringing the pure and potent message of Godhead, flavored with the sweetness of your unique transcendental personality. This world is wrapped in ignorance of Kṛṣṇa, and the tales of woe, misfortune, and havoc are ever mounting. The heavy repercussions for our lives of sin constituted our future. But you intervened, carrying the divine power to grant us pardon and parole.

The Mayavāda doctrine, sprouted in the soil of Bhārata-varṣa and spreading to Western shores, consumed the intellectuals in its hypnotic trance of monism and nihilism. It then seeped into the minds of the masses, like a dose of poison in the water supply. With awesome philosophical power and clarity, you have revealed Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, as the Absolute Truth, delivering a deathblow to impersonalism and voidism.

Beyond liberation, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us pure devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the most sublime activity. You have responded to Lord Caitanya’s plea for assistance in distributing *Kṛṣṇa-prema* as one else has ever done. The miracle of your attachment in creating ISKCON is like a many-faceted gem; although pondered and contemplated continuously, it reveals ever new depth and brilliance.

We, your disciples and granddisciples, fervently pray that we may eternally remain within the shelter of your lotus feet.

## Portugal

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this most sacred day, celebrating your divine appearance in this world, we humbly request that you smile mercifully upon our endeavor to glorify you and express our feelings of deep gratitude.

To properly glorify you is not possible for any ordinary soul still struggling with the illusory attraction of *māyā.* You are Kṛṣṇa’s mercy manifestation in this world and like Kṛṣṇa cannot be easily understood or appreciated. But as the Supreme Lord can be understood by one whose eyes have been anointed with the salve of unalloyed love and devotion, we are also praying for the opportunity to become totally surrendered in loving service at your lotus feet. Perhaps then we could properly comprehend and sufficiently glorify your qualities and your contribution to the suffering humanity—-this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

However, until such devotion actually manifests itself in our vacant hearts we feel compelled to simply quote your own words in our effort to glorify you. Your greatness is an open secret revealed within the pages of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā,* and your countless letters to your disciples. Your purports and letters are full of purity and clarity and firmly establish the glory of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya. As a pure devotee, preaching relentlessly to eradicate our illusion and give us Kṛṣṇa, you glorified the Lord so sweetly. But simultaneously within your words of praise for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa you have made clear the greatness of one who give Kṛṣṇa, and therefore we choose to safely repeat like a parrot what you have written. As one worships the Ganges River by offering water drawn from the Ganges or one who worships the sun by offering a small lamp, we simply offer your words in an effort to praise you.

This country of Portugal, religiously pious but spiritually starving, is now for the first time coming into contact with your unadulterated presentation of the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* and thus far the response has been extremely receptive. At the present moment we do not have too many books, so following your example we go out each day to the center of the city in Lisbon and simply chant the holy name. The result of our tiny three-man chanting party is that afterwards many people, both young and old, find their way back to our little apartment with a very sincere desire to know more about the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The seeds are being planted now for the first time, or as you said yourself, “The poison has already entered.” Clearly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are the most significant gift the world has ever received.

In one letter in 1976 you wrote, “Over the past ten years I have given the framework, and now we have become more than the British Empire. Even the British Empire is not so expansive as we. They had only a portion of the world, and we have not completed expanding. We must expand unlimitedly more and more. But I must remind you that I have to complete the translation of the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,* which will be our lasting contribution to the world.”

The *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* is described in the First Canto: “This *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* is the literary incarnation of God and is compiled by Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the incarnation of God. It is meant for the ultimate good of all people and it is all-successful, all-blissful, and all-perfect.” Within the purport you explain, “One can derive from the study of the *Bhagavatam* all the benefits that are to be derived from the personal presence of the Lord. It carries with it all the transcendental blessings of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa that we expect from His personal contact.” In another purport you say, “We must therefore accept *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* as the direct representative of the Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. One who can see *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* can see also Lord Kṛṣṇa in person. They are identical.”

Prior to your divine appearance in this world, Śrīla Prabhupāda, such direct association with the Lord was locked within the Sanskrit language and the lofty commentaries of the *ācāryas.* The only contact the Western world had with either the *Bhagavatam* or the *Bhagavad-gītā* was comparable to milk touched by the lips of a serpent. The poisonous effects were seen in the form of widespread impersonalist and voidist philosophy.

Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the “light of the *Bhagavata”* would have remained covered by the clouds of Kali-yuga. As the glory of the *Bhagavatam* is unlimited, your glory as the personal medium through which the whole world now has access to the “spotless *Purāṇa* is also unlimited.

As we read and distribute your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we realize our total dependence upon you now in the form of your instructions in your books. With the passing of time, realization of our debt to you increases. You have given us everything required to return home, back to Godhead. In one letter to the devotees in the Los Angeles community you wrote, “If you at all feel indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay the spiritual master. The real ocean of mercy is Kṛṣṇa, and it is the duty of the spiritual master to tell the disciple to come to the ocean and be happy. I am trying my best, and if you follow surely you will benefit.”

Please give us the strength, courage, and conviction to follow strictly your example and instructions in the form of your books to the millions of lost souls suffering in this miserable world of birth and death. If you kindly bless us with such service then perhaps someday we will be able to understand to some small degree the true significance of your divine appearance in this material world.

*Your most fallen servants,*

Praghoṣa dāsa / Kuladeva dāsa / Anantadeva dāsa / Bhakta Ronalda / Bhaktin Carmin / Bhaktin Maria

## Puerto Rico

On this most auspicious day let us remember that only because of your merciful appearance, are we, who are so fallen, able to observe VYĀSA PŪJĀ specifically in commemoration of Your Divine Grace. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can never account for this great mercy that you are giving us every day. You have picked us up, and you are constantly guiding us and constantly remaining with us as our well-wisher. How can we ever repay you? Just as the ground is the only support for those whose feet have slipped, so you are the only divine shelter even for those like ourselves who have committed offenses unto you. There can be no greater offender than myself, and yet you’re still so kind and forgiving that you always extend your loving hand. Yes, it’s this love we cannot comprehend; it is not available in this dark, forsaken world full of voidism and materialism. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have so kindly lifted this cloud and helped us rise above this great obstacle; have pointed out the way showing us Kṛṣṇa. You’ve taken this great burden and contending with a load of sinners, constantly being inconvenienced by us, you never leave our side. How can your deep compassion be understood?

*he guro jñāna-da-dina-bandho*  
*svānanda-dātā karuṇaika-sindho*  
*vṛndāvanāsīna hitāvatāra*  
*prasīda rādhā-praṇāya-pracāra*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like myself, and you are preaching the divine love of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon us, for we are always hoping for your mercy and kindness as we have no other shelter than the aegis of your lotus feet. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us all to cooperate and assist one another in serving your will, which is nondifferent from the will of Kṛṣṇa. You’ve stated that in cooperating in this way, this will be the proof of our love for you. Should you help us remember you, then all inauspicious is produced. May your name and glories always be chanted. Begging to remain eternally under your constant guidance and your lotus feet.

*Your humble servants of New Govardhana Hill,*

## Queens

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākaayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Just as Arjuna, after seeing Lord Kṛṣṇa’s universal form, offers Him obeisances, may we attempt to imitate in similar manner.

*Namaḥ purastād atha pṛṣṭhatas te*  
*Namo ‘stu te sarvata eva sarva*  
*Ananta-vīryāmita-vikramas tvaṁ*  
*Sarvaṁ samāpnoṣi tato ‘si sarvaḥ*  
*(Bg. 11.40)*

**Obeisances from the front, from behind, and from all Sides! O unbounded power, you are the master of limit-Less might! You are all-pervading, and thus you are Everything!**

O dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our eternal obeisances at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet! At least in the imitating mood of Arjuna, where he says to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa:

*Sakheti matvā prasabhaṁ yad uktaṁ*  
*He Kṛṣṇa he yādava he sakheti*  
*Ajānatā mahimānaṁ tavedaṁ*  
*Mayā pramādāt praṇayena vāpi*  
*(Bg. 11.41)*

He says that he has in the past addressed Him, without thinking, as “O Kṛṣṇa, O Yādava, O my friend,” just as he might have done in madness or in love without knowing His real glory. *Tat kṣāmaye tvām aham aprameyam.* “Excuse me for all my immeasurable offenses.”

Your Divine Grace is explaining in the purport, “But Kṛṣṇa is so kind and merciful, that in spite of such opulence He played with Arjuna as a friend. Such is the transcendental loving reciprocation between the devtoee and the Lord. The relationship between the living entity and Kṛṣṇa is fixed eternally; it cannot be forgotten, as we can see from the behavior of Arjuna.”

This is also absolutely true in the relationship eternally established between us and Your Divine Grace. And so the prayers of Arjuna go on. There is no end to the absolute insignificance of a disciple in front of his Guru Maharaja, and therefore it is always amazing to observe your mercy as a focal point being reestablished from time to time despite all sorts of difficulties arising in your established movement, even in your permanent physical absence.

To please you in your service to Kṛṣṇa is our life and soul. You had a long-cherished desire to worship Śrī Nāthajī. It’s becoming a reality; yet it is extremely difficult to understand without your mercy how Lord Śrī Nāthajī is gradually appearing in your movement just over the course of three years! How can we be worthy of your mercy without being properly purified? And how can we be properly purified without Your Divine Grace’s mercy? It is described as simply causeless! Therefore, please let us imitate at least, as in Arjuna’s words for Śrī Kṛṣṇa, O Gurudeva, *na tvat-samo ‘sty abhyadhikaḥ kuto ‘nyo loka-traye ‘py apratima-prabhāva* (Bg. 11.43). Except for Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself and His other expansions, no one is equal to you, nor can anyone be equal to you within these three worlds. You are immeasurable.

*Tasmāt praṇamya praṇidhāya kāyaṁ*  
*Prasādaye tvām aham īśam īḍyam*  
*Piteva putrasya sakheva sakhyuḥ*  
*Priyaḥ priyāyārhasi deva soḍhum*  
*(Bg 11.44)*

Therefore, O beloved Guru Maharaja, you are the worshipable lord for your disciples. Thus we fall down to offer you are respects and ask for your mercy. Please tolerate the wrongs that we may have done to you and bear with us as a father with his sons, or a friend with his friend, or a lover with his beloved.

*Your unworthy sons of ISKCON Queens, New Nathadwar*

## Rio de Janeiro

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances hundreds and millions of times at your lotus feet! Upon this most auspicious occasion of your holy appearance day, we pray for you to simply help us understand and properly appreciate your mission.

Even if we had thousands of mouths, they would be fruitless to explain your glories. You are the universal teacher (*jagad-guru),* and alone you started the transformation of the world, touching the lives of millions through your personal presence, your books, your temples, and your disciples.

You are like an ocean of mercy, and you have overflooded the shores of that ocean, blessing all the world with the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Because you are so merciful and kind to all the suffering living entities, you have given your while life to deliver all the fallen souls in the same way as did Vasudeva Datta.

*jīvera duḥkha dekhi ‘mora hṛdaya bidare*  
*sarva-jīvera pāpa prabhu deha’ mora śire*  
*jīvera pāpa lañā mūyi karoṅ naraka bhoga*  
*sakala jīvera prabhu ghucāha bhava-roga*

**“O my Lord, my heart breaks to see the suffering of all conditioned souls; therefore I request You to transfer the** ***karma*** **of their sinful lives upon my head. O my Lord, let me suffer perpetually in a hellish condition, accepting all the sinful reactions of all living entities. Please finish their diseased material life. (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **15. 162-63)**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, enable us to understand the kindness of Lord Caitanya in the form of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement because we have no eyes to see or heart to feel. And although we are most dull we are begging for the shade of your lotus feet.

All glories to Śrīla Gurudeva, the *jagad-guru.* May we somehow learn to accept your mercy.

*Your eternal servants at Rio de Janeiro Mandira, Brazil,*

## St.Louis

It’s six-thirty in the morning. The birds sing sweetly, and peacocks sound their exotic meow. The air resounds with *japa,* chanted by your faithful disciples. Two devotees softly sing the *Brahma-saṁhitā* on the outer veranda, upstairs in your Vṛndāvana quarters, waiting. You ring the bell, and one devotee enters the inner veranda, offers obeisances, and enters the mosquito net on the bed to massage your heart. You speak softly about ISKCON matters, Bombay, Mayapur, New Vrindaban, etc. Management is *arcanam,* you once said. You servant helps you to your bath and personally bathes your transcendental form. Then he brings you to the indoor room. You sit on a rocking chair as he helps you with your *kurtā,* and you apply *tilaka* with your Kṛṣṇa Balarama compact. The other servant changes the linens and brings the bed on the outer veranda with pillows, scented garland, bouquet, handkerchief, *loṭā,* incense, and *cāmara.* He had also brought fresh cloth and *kaupīna* for you. You are then aided to the outer veranda where you sit on the bed, garlanded by your loving disciples. The incense is sweet as one devotee fans you with a *cāmara,* in a figure-eight fashion, not for cooling but for keeping the numerous flies from your thin form. A few *sannyāsīs* come, and you speak to them shortly. One is sent to Nepal and told a story of having the courage of an Englishman and the heart of a Bengali mother. A letter is read from Śrī Lanka. Your disciples have challenged the scientists: you create one fly, one mouse, or one cat, and we’ll give you so many *lakh* rupees; otherwise you must pay. You approve heartily. Another letter from two of your men from Sweden traveling in Yugoslavia, following your program of festival, feast, and book distribution. Fifteen hundred books sold and the people all left with stomachs full, hands full, and purses empty. You laugh strongly and reply. “This news is making me forget my disease.”

All the devotees go except for the fly chaser, and you close your eyes for a very long time, silently taking in the August Vṛndāvana breeze and reflecting on the news. The attendant constantly fans, and from time to time you ask for the top electric fan on, or the spittoon, or a desired disciple. At different times the *kavirāja* is announced, and before he comes you motion for your socks and your *cādar* to cover your lap. He has requested you to wear them constantly, and you act like you are doing just that. By nine-thirty, devotees come up the stairs with a *tulasī* garland and help you into your rocking chair palanquin. As they carry you down the stairs for *darśana,* the *guru-kula* boys *kīrtana* all around you. As you enter the temple room, you chant, “Nitāi-Gaura, Radhe Śyāma, *jaya* Kṛṣṇa-Balarama.” After greeting the Deities, you are brought under the *tamala* tree as sixty or so devotees chant at your feet and the *guru-kula* boys dance before you. You motion to your assistants to give the boys the *tulasi* and other fragrant garlands as you mercifully glance upon the entire scene. Soon you are carried back to your ground room by the *dayitas,* and you spend the day meeting with guests, *kavirājas,* and other disciples. Massage is also given during the day. At about seven-thirty you are again carried upstairs to your bed in the inner veranda, where an elder disciple reads from the *Caitanya-caritāṁṛta* or *Śrīmad-bhagavatam* with a small lamp. You talk of going to London and America to give the Western devotees a chance to be strengthened. Please strengthen me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Take me with you please. Allow me to do something nice for you and enter into your transcendental pastimes.

*Your eternal servants,*

## San Diego

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We humbly offer our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance. In this present decadent age, where the demoniac class of men are given all fame and notoriety, we proudly embrace the opportunity to loudly proclaim the transcendental qualities of your divine self and, thus, give praise where it is actually due.

Prior to your appearance in the Western world, we were completely oblivious of the standard for qualifying ourselves as human beings, what to speak of having any idea of, or aspiration to attain, the position of pure devotion to God. Our heroes at that time were the widely acclaimed rock stars and speculative philosophers whose lives, unbeknownst to us, revealed nothing more than their gross ignorance of reality. Befooled and absorbed in their illusory conceptions, we were all, thus, diving headlong into the depths of sense gratification, blindly waving the banners of our doomed leaders, ourselves destined for the same unfortunate fate as they: “Whatever action is performed by a great man, common men follow in their footsteps. And whatever standard he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues.” (Bg. 3.21)

Only when you opened our eyes to our actual situation as Kṛṣṇa’s parts and parcels did we realize our misplaced allegiance and our true position as the eternal servants of Your Divine Grace. No longer would we allow ourselves to be cheated, for we were finally being shown the real meaning of truth, of freedom, and of love; and you gave us a sense of purpose and optimism that we had never before known to exist. Realizing the value of these priceless gifts, we must beg you, firstly, for humility amongst us; secondly, for the necessary tolerance; thirdly, for freedom from the desire for false prestige; and, fourthly, for the ability to offer one another all respect.

*tṛṇād api sunīcena*  
*taror iva sahiṣṇunā*  
*amāninā mānadena*  
*kīrtanīyah sadā hariḥ*

We understand fully that only by serving together in this manner can we attain the unity necessary for victory.

Because you are directly empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, Your Divine Grace has made the goal and the process of devotional service easy to understand. Fallen as we are, however, it oftentimes seems nearly impossible for us to maintain that cherished enthusiasm to act on that understanding. Therefore, we must also beg to remember to constantly and fervently pray at your lotus feet to help us intensify this enthusiasm more and more so that we can be blessed with the will to cast aside all of our material considerations and, thus, help make your movement, and our lives, a success.

Jaya Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Maharaja Prabhupada *ki jaya!*

*We beg to remain…*

Your fallen servants at ISKCON San Diego (New Govardhana-dhāma)

## San Francisco

*Our dear beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We beg you to give us determination to continue the path we have begun in your service here in San Francisco. We simply want to live as you instructed, and thereby attract the intelligent class of men to your lotus feet. Because of our past miseducation, we are finding it very difficult to be the well-wisher of everyone as you instructed. We often find ourselves falling down into the miserable pit of fault-finding with one another, our superiors, and those needing our help. But we pray to remain determined in your path, to develop some love and trust within ourselves, and start practicing the same with our brothers and sisters and whomever else we meet.

Please give us faith so we do not resort to short-cut solutions to our day-to-day pressing problems, and instead apply Kṛṣṇa consciousness in full and responsibly. Please give us knowledge so that we can avoid the dictates of our mind and do as you have done. Please give us patience so that we can bear one another’s shortcomings and trust that we each will grow under your care. Please give us understanding so that we can conquer our doubts with appreciation and love, And most importantly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep the memories of your pastime fresh in our minds and hearts so that we can remain strong and determined forever on this path.

*Your servants,*  
Bhaktivedanta Fellowship  
Bhaktivedanta Institute

## San Salvador

*Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet; accept our humble VYĀSA PŪJĀ offering on this glorious day during which we lovingly celebrate your appearance. On this auspicious day in which we are commemorating the initiation of your spiritual pastimes in this material world, we wish to repeatedly make known our unconditional adherence to the unified effort of all the Vaisnava devotees of the Lord who are anxious to satisfy your holy will to take all the conditioned souls back home.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the personification of mercy. You are going from door to door in the big cities as well as in the most remote towns and villages. You are going there through the instructions contained in your books. Your spiritual potency is so great that in spite of our being so fallen, you have engaged us in the fulfillment of the prophetic order of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“The chanting of My name will be heard in every town and village.”**

This prophecy is being fulfilled through your empowered associates who are always encouraging us to continue struggling day after day against the strong illusion. We are experiencing at this precise moment in the real life drama of our zone (a minute portion of this Martya-loka) that in any place and at any moment, in the midst of the typical ways of Kali-yuga and to the rhythm of tremendous bombs, bullets, and sirens of the ambulances that speed here and there, one can hear the transcendental sound of the *mṛdaṅga* and *karatalas,* intoning the holy names of the Lord. The people here are amazed by the power of this great *sankirtana* movement, reflected in the valiant mood of its devotees. For this reason, although impoverished and embittered by the cruel war, they are accepting your transcendental books. One of the eleven *vartamāna-ācāryas* of our disciplic succession in regard to this connected that in such a place and situation it is very easy to realize your *Bhagavad-gītā* As It Is, since at every step one encounters many dead wherever he goes, forcing him to become like the sage, who being transcendental situated laments neither for the living nor the dead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because we are on a path which is full of adversities and contrasts, we have felt weakened on many occasions; nevertheless, because you, as the ideal *aśārya,* have taught us by your example, we have been able to recover our strength at the very moment that we *paṇḍala* in Bombay in March of 1977. You were on your *vyāsāsana,* and in the beginning you were sustained almost exclusively by pillows and cushions, being weakened by the harassment of the reactions of our impious activities. You were already ill, but as soon as you began to preach, addressing a large audience, you sat up like a lion ready to spring upon its prey.

On more than one occasion we have experienced the discouragement and fatigue; nevertheless, upon evoking the remembrance of such moving examples, exhibited only by one who is directly empowered by the Supreme Lord, we have been able to emerge victorious even in the midst of the greatest difficulty.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can understand from your eloquent examples, chastisements, anecdotes, and your Bhaktivedanta purports, which beautifully decorate your transcendental books, that your didactic objective is to make all of the conditioned souls understand their constitutional position. Therefore we conclude that you are truly the power behind this movement. If you were not maintaining us surely everything would crumble to the ground. We know that Lord Kṛṣṇa is assisting you at every moment; that is to say, He is our ally, and therefore the success of this great is secure. Actually, you have done everything; we are simply minute instruments in your dexterous hands.

For many thousands of reasons we have accepted to follow you blindly, and we are begging you with folded hands that you will allow us to continue upon this path—illumined by the understanding of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa.

*Your humble servants from San Salvador Mandira,*

## Santa Cruz

*Dear Gurudeva,*

We are wondering how people so little as us could write something in glorification of your greatness, your life being a shining example to your disciples and to the world. You love Kṛṣṇa so much, that it is difficult to explain. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are Kṛṣṇa’s dearest, most intimate devotee, and on His request, you came to the material world to deliver us from the pangs of material existence. O master of masters, you are so exalted that we only wish to take your instructions as our life and soul. Your Divine Grace, if you will examine us, you will find no good qualities; your mercy keeps us going.

O father, at your feet all masters sit. You are like the touchstone; whoever came in contact with you left being favorable to Kṛṣṇa. We owe you a great debt; this debt is impossible to pay. Even if we serve you for millions of births, we would not be able to pay back a fraction of this debt. You gave us the matchless gift, and we are so unqualified, that we don’t know how to accept it. We only wish we could be as loyal to our spiritual master as you to yours. On this auspicious day, we cherish the opportunity and privilege of offering these prayers of gratitude to you, our eternal father, guide, and well-wisher.

Dear Guru, it had been only a few years since you enabled us to take spiritual birth. You are a *mahā-bhāgavata,* the greatest devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. From the time of your arrival in the West in 1965, your writings have clearly established your life’s mission.

Oh, swanlike person, you are the personification of the Lord’s mercy. How we came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and made progress day by day is only due to your unlimited potency. Just like the sun, which can separate all good elements from dirt, you were able to find whatever little spark of desire for Kṛṣṇa that was left in us and made it into a blazing fire.

You are so expert in spreading devotional service that you give us millions of opportunities to serve you when you were personally present in your beautiful divine form.

Therefore we offer our respectful obeisances unto you, we take shelter at your lotus feet, O you whose glories are difficult to understand. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your small servants in Santa Cruz,*

## Santiago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having surrendered at His lotus feet.**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

For someone to be dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa is not something ordinary, which can be obtained by some material cause, but only by full surrender in unalloyed devotional service, like you demonstrated through your chanting, preaching, service, etc. All your activities were definitely a true expression of how intimately attached you are to Kṛṣṇa.

Your whole life was an example to be followed by your disciples. Especially the more advanced devotees, who had your association in the form of *vapuh,* have taken the nectar of your preaching and are continuing with the *sankirtana* movement which you initiated at the time when Western society was so degraded. And above all, you did everything all by yourself, without help from anyone.

It’s only because of the desire to please your spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa that you could rise above all obstacles to bring Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West. The enthusiasm, determination, and patience which you have shown in order to expand this movement is what we desire intensely. Please use us as your instruments in your magnificent mission.

*Bhagavad-gītā* explains that common men follow in the footsteps of a great man. And therefore we want to attain that pure state by which we can present you as you are.

For the sake of saving the fallen living entities in this world, you abandoned everything—-wife, children, occupation, friends, even your most beloved residence in Vṛndāvana.

All we pray for is that one day we can render our body, mind, and words as insignificant particles at your lotus feet and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness with great anxiety for the rest of our lives. We call upon the mercy and protection of our elder Godbrothers to always empower us to satisfy you, no matter where we are, no matter what time it is, no matter how difficult things may be.

*Your eternal servants,*  
Santiago Mandira

## Santo Domingo

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. Being so fallen, how can we glorify you, who are beyond our small minds. On this VYĀSA PŪJĀ day it is indeed an honor that you have allowed *caṇḍālas* like ourselves to take shelter of you through this prayer. Your causeless mercy to us in Santo Domingo can only be compared to a swan who sets down in a dark, dirty place, being full of the light of knowledge and purity. We were forced to gather the dust falling from your lotus feet and sprinkle it on our head thereby replacing the stool with brains by which to follow your instructions. We were forced to gather the dust falling from your lotus feet and sprinkle it on our head thereby replacing the stool with brains by which to follow your instructions. Your instructions, of course, when properly heard and remembered can purify even *mlecchas* like ourselves. Your inexhaustible literary works can be compared to an ocean. Just like a flame can consume any amount of gasoline supplied to it, your transcendental books can consume any amount of ignorance supplied to them; and I must say ignorance we seem to have in abundance here in Santo Domingo. You have done so much in such a short time that you have left us in awe just at the recitation of your name, helping us to understand the verse:

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sabdhiḥ*

The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord. Just to remember your form, name, activities, and desires enthuses our mind and hearts, giving us strength to preach the message of Lord Caitanya. This is the most blessed event because this day we can especially remember the greatest exponent of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the world. You appeared in this world under the order of Lord Kṛṣṇa, a pure servant breathing the fire of knowledge and dissipating the darkness of ignorance. We here in Santo Domingo bow down at your lotus feet. Your act of giving the residents of Santo Domingo your mercy and shelter is proof of your glorious position of Patita-pāvana: the deliverer of the fallen, lord and master of the devotees. Please, bless us this day with special potency to become first-class book distributors and preachers under your ISKCON and to never leave the shelter of your lotus feet. On this VYĀSA PŪJĀ day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we ask: Just let us be a part of your *līlā.* What more can we ask for?

*Your servants at Santo Domingo Yātrā,*

## Saudi Arabia

After viewing various billboards, Your Divine Grace in 1969  
Told this insignificant fool to put “Match  
This, All Religions” on a sign.

As with all of VYĀSA PŪJĀ’s  
Written and spoken philosophy  
This one statement contains much wisdom,  
Nectar and prophecy.

It is a challenge for a world of quarrel  
And dispute  
Translated as an imperative into common  
Language for the fallen.

The purport is that your divine teachings  
Up to standard we must present  
And in our places as “ācāryas”  
Your Divine Grace we represent.

We are proud to attempt a gift in every  
Way greater than religion,  
And we know that the platform  
Highest in human life is religion.

Having accepted “this” form Your Divine  
Grace, in relationship absorbed,  
As individuals and as ISKCON we distribute  
“this” Kṛṣṇa-sambandha;

Distribution causes “this” to grow  
With Hare Kṛṣṇa culture,  
And as bhakti-latā, entwines us in  
Your Divine Grace’s loving service.

If others do not come or if some have  
Left, we discriminate,  
Demons, demoniac life and hell from  
Devotion, God and innocent;

Hells and demons, although a part of  
Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,  
Challenge us to become your pure servants,  
Convince us to try to catch the innocent.

On this VYĀSA PŪJĀ day an insignificant fool  
Prays to qualify for your divine mercy.  
Let the brine be purified from these  
Salty tears to make a sweet bath for  
The senior Godbrothers to bathe your  
Divine lotus feet.

*ISKCON Saudi Arabia,*

Dayānanda dāsa, Nandarāṇī-devī-dasi, Govinda dāsa

## Seattle

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most fallen obeisances at your two lotus feet. Somehow or other, you have again given us the opportunity to pay homage to you on the glorious occasion of your appearance day. Despite the fact that we have made numerous offenses and mistakes, you have mercifully tolerates us, and you have allowed us to remain within the kingdom of ISKCON. What can be said of your causeless mercy upon us? This past year has been one of intense pressure upon us: pressure to continue to serve you, to remember you and pressure to follow your instructions. This pressure is necessary because without it we will lapse into a comalike existence, eating and sleeping in your temple, not conscious of the real meaning of spiritual life. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to apply pressure on us, for if you let up on us, we will surely float away, being carried by the waves of *māyā.*

We must exist on the instructions you have given us. Most of us did not have such intimate, personal association with you when you so wonderfully blessed us by your physical presence. Yet, that does not seem to make so much of a difference now because we are realizing more and more that actual intimacy with you is not necessarily experienced by one’s material proximity to you, but rather, as the years progress and as our spiritual life intensifies, the relationship with you becomes closer and deeper, by how much we, the servants, serve the master—the spiritual master. “Leader,” you told us, “means one who is a first-class disciple. *Evam paramparā-prāptam.* One who is perfectly following. Our instruction is, *guru-mukha-padma-vākya.* Do you know this?”

Sometimes, due to our covered consciousness, we stray from the path you have made for us, and we become bewildered as to which way we are to proceed. Should we maintain the temples you started at all cost? Should we double book distribution every year? Should we see that our Godbrothers and Godsisters are happily engaged in your service and that everything is all right with them? When you were on the planet personally instructing us, the answer to these questions was quite clear: Yes!

We are humbly begging you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to continue to pressure us, because we are feeling your caring presence by that pressure and, like children who will do all nonsense in the absence of their parents but who will shape up when the parents are around, we will shape up and get back to the business you instructed us to do and then the path back home, back to Godhead will again become crystal clear. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your worthless servants,*

**Temple**

## Śrī Lanka

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākaayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my** ***guru,*** **my spiritual master, opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my spiritual master, opened by eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

WE, Your disciples and granddisciples in Śrī Lanka fall at your feet. We feel very inadequate and fallen; yet you have kindly allowed us to engage in a service which is dear to you. By your mercy alone, we can see the noble purpose of life. We may try to understand your position or your glories with our limited mind as your sublime qualities are like those of Kṛṣṇa. Not only were these same qualities manifest in yourself during your *līlā,* but you in turn have invested these same qualities in your pure representatives who are presently infusing this planet with love of God. Simply serving, hearing or associating with these great souls will yield the perfection of human life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most merciful representative of the Supreme Lord. We were simply suffering in the blazing fire of material existence until you so kindly appeared, bringing water from the ocean of mercy. As the confidential servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you have propagated the *sankirtana* movement throughout the entire planet, bringing the most valuable gift in all the three worlds, “the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.” Your transcendental position is so exalted that we cannot calculate your greatness. Even though we are sinful and low-born you have made us your menial servants and engaged us in assisting your preaching mission. Please give us the strength to carry on your preaching work and to be always fully engaged in the *sankirtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*Your servants in Śrī Lanka,*

Mahākartā dāsa brahmacari / Viṣṇupāda dāsa brahmacari / Kāliya-Kṛṣṇa dāsa brahmacari / Śacīsuta dāsa brahmacari / Rantideva dāsabrahamcari / Rāja-Rāma dāsa brahmacari / Śacīnandana dāsa brahmacari / Vrajeśvara dāsa adhikārī / Bhakta Kulendran / Bhakta Karalasingham / Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī / Rasapūrṇā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Vijitta / Bhaktin Parameshwary / Bhaktin Satkuneswary

## State College

*Dear Master,*

All glories to you, the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead! You are the confidential servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Through your grace, the devotees are feeling bliss, even in the midst of this dark age of Kali.

Unfortunately, I don’t actually have realization of your spiritual glories, and it is useless to delude myself, straining with these blind eyes. But may I submit one hopeful entreaty? I pray to be able to step slowly, respectfully, cautiously into the foyer of your transcendental pastimes, like a street urchin who has somehow, inconceivably, been invited into a royal ball at the magnanimous behest of some great personality. May I engage my vision in seeing first your manifested pastimes in your *Līlāmṛta* and through the realizations of your senior disciples, who understood what you were saying and what you wanted. May I please come to understand what you were saying and what you wanted. May I please come to understand very clearly what you were saying and doing with your ISKCON mission and exactly what you want me to do for you.

Sometimes this mission of yours has frightened me, and I thought that I would rather see something else: “Couldn’t I see glorious things? Must I see all the struggle, work, lack of support, and apparent diversity?”

Did I –do I – secretly cherish that one day I would be like you—respected, popular, learned, a renouncer of the world, the topmost transcendentalist worshiped on a golden throne, the savior of the world? But whose secret longing would be to work long hours into the night writing, after longer days of haggling with printers in a shabby and primitive bazaars (before breakfast), walking again and again and again to try to distribute spiritual literatures to smug materialists, with all their hyped-up self confidence in so many concocted and implausible plans—all of which could only appear ludicrous to any learned person? Unsalaried and unsupported even by family and Godbrothers, working for the pleasure and glory of someone else, and always thinking oneself the servant of the servant of the servant—who would cherish this position?

I must confess that my mind has sometimes been terrified at trying to seriously relate to such a life, but somehow, inexplicably, I am coming to understand how you could do these things, really very easily, by virtue of your loving relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa. And I am beginning to understand a little more what this means in our relationship with you.

There is nothing material about your relationship with any being. There is no exploitation to be found, because your relationship with us is based on Lord Kṛṣṇa’s relationship with us. Kṛṣṇa is eternally, ever-increasingly the well-wishing friend of every living being (no matter how fallen they may be), never ceasing to provide for them, rehabilitate them, and reciprocate with them as they desire, right up to becoming their son or friend or lover. And you are His dear friend and confidential servant, acting in this world on a merciful mission to please your Lord and relieve His distress over the position of the entangled, conditioned *jīvas* in the material energy.

Therefore you establish relationships with the *jīvas* designed to remind them of your (and their) dearest friend, of His qualities, name, pastimes, etc., and to help them recover from the disease of wanting to be separate from Him. Knowing of this vast reservoir of love (*bhakti-rasa)* which Kṛṣṇa wants to exchange with each *jīva,* and which each *jīva* is ardently seeking (though in all wrong ways), you work tirelessly to bring about their happy reunion. What could be a more worthwhile endeavor? What other motivation could there possibly be for your tireless, diligent, tolerant, expert and merciful pastimes?

Dear master, I wish that I could be conscious of even a drop of that ocean of love that exists on the spiritual plane, so pure and free from envy. Then perhaps I could go from *jīva* to *jīva,* bubbling forth effusively with the transcendental broadcast of the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa, stunning them with realized accounts of His transcendental name, qualities, and pastimes and sincerely wishing for the reunion of every one of them with Him from the bottom of my heart.

But actually I am so fallen and hardhearted I can scarcely dream of what real love must be like. One author once said, “Hell is not being able to love.” I have spent much time in this hell. I can only hope that someday, by your grace, I can become free from this painful envy. Please forgive me for being such an offense to you, to your devotees, and to your Lord. I seem incapable of being proper, according to Vedic injunctions, and when I hear the qualifications of the great souls, like yourself or Arjuna, from the *śāstras,* then I feel like a hopeless animal. Therefore I can understand that your mercy is all that I am made of.

You are the sum total of the thirty-three million demigods, for you are the confidential servitor of Lord Kṛṣṇa. I do not know how you could ever be pleased with me. Can it be that you actually care for one so fallen as I?

By your grace we are understanding that we cannot do *anything* to please you, and also, simultaneously, that you have independently agreed to be pleased with our humble endeavor to stay here and preach. This revelation is your gift, that we may accept our acceptance by you (which is very difficult for me, an unacceptable, to believe!). You are the source of our faith (which is not just a belief, an intellectual act produced by will and emotion, but which is Kṛṣṇa’s blessings, as described in *Bhagavad-gītā,* coming through you), and by your grace we know that if we simply goon following your instructions and trying sincerely to serve you, that Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is actually being satisfied by this. In this way we are assured that all of our disqualifications will be overcome by your causeless mercy and that we shall be able to return to our home, back to Godhead. All glories to you, kind lord and master!

*Your eternal servants,*

Stambha dāsa brahmacari / Līlānanda dāsa adhikārī / Bhakta Glen / Bhakta Mike

## Stockholm

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we are praying that our love and devotion for you will develop and increase unlimitedly, that we will never deviate from your instructions, and that we will realize in truth this Vaisnava philosophy, which is identical with the eternal religion, the *sanātana-dharma,* of the *jīva.*

At a time when so-called scientific atheism, utopian communism, and wishy-washy impersonalism were deluding the world and keeping it in the darkness of ignorance, you came to teach the highest love of God and deliver the ultimate knowledge. Who are we to deserve such mercy?

Linked in the chain of disciplic succession, you are certainly very different from the religious leaders of the world, whose teachings are bound to be limited by human alterations and sectarian considerations. You have perfectly delivered the eternal truth and deepest love of God in accordance with time and circumstances. And everyone, devotee or demon, has been affected by your presence. We are convinced that although this Kṛṣṇa conscious movement is comparatively small by worldly estimation, it has had more influence on the present situation of the world than anything else. If it weren’t for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the condition of the world would have been much worse, and we would’ve already been living in hell.

It is by your potency alone that this movement is still going on and is increasing, and you have said that nothing can stop this great force from outside. Only by our own falldown it can be checked. Of course, we have no strength on our own, and therefore we pray that you will keep us on the path of purity, so that we will not be motivated by false prestige and personal considerations and thereby ruin everything, like so many religious movements previously have been ruined by useless followers.

Where pure love, lasting happiness, and knowledge are becoming manifest, that’s where the religion is being practiced. It is all very obvious. No one can disagree. We are therefore confident that we are standing at the dawn of the greatest religious revolution in history, and it is all due to your unlimited mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

## Switzerland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. Today is again your appearance day. Its coming causes your presence to be felt ever more intensely by your devotees and admirers, reminding us of the enormous debt which we shall perpetually owe Your Divine Grace.

For the tenth time (how quickly time has passed!) it is my great fortune to compose some meager words of praise for your satisfaction. Astonishingly, it still seems only a short while ago that I obtained your blue-covered MacMillan edition of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and began reading it, at first with curiosity, then with aroused attention, and finally with the conviction that this book was about to irrevocably change the course of my life. Upon completing it, I instinctively turned again to the front cover and intently scrutinizing for a long time the words “by A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami.” “Who is this sainted person from whose heart and mind such nectarean ‘purports’ have sprung?” I excitedly thought. I exulted in the realization that such a spiritual giant exists in this modern world of petty-minded dwarfs. My heart quickened as an intense desire to one day become like you overwhelmed me. “Yes,” I thought, “I shall find out this rare soul and learn to become like him.” I have never regretted that decision for even a second, although I am very sorry to be such a slow-footed and dull-witted learner.

It is no exaggeration to proclaim that your glories are unlimited because so is your devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Qualitatively and quantitatively it has far excelled any conditioned soul’s miserable attempt to appreciate and laud it adequately. Some fools may disparagingly comment about Vyāsa-pūjā offerings: “What purpose is served by such flattery?” But there can be no question of flattering you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, since there is no possibility of exaggerating the transcendental qualities of your flawless devotion to the Personality of Godhead. “Never mind you would say when fools criticized Guara-Nitāi’s *sankirtana* movement, “the dogs bark, but the caravan passes.” How appropriately these words apply to your own towering achievements: your transcendental BBT literatures, ISKCON temples, preaching centers, *guru-kulas, prasadam* distribution programs, *nagara-kīrtana* parties, and staunch Vaisnava followers.

A privileged few have known Your Divine Grace, and the significance of your most blessed appearance. Please empower all your faithful followers to remedy this intolerable situation by transforming the privileged few into the privileged many until every last conditioned soul, his eyes brimming with tears of ecstasy, can cry “*Jaya* Prabhupada, *jaya* Prabhupada!”

*Your insignificant servants,*
