## Sydney

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Following the instructions of your spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura, your determination to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness makes the determination of the little sparrow who was trying to dry up the ocean seem very weak in comparison.

You are the personification of determination. You never doubted the success of your mission. Because it was the order of your spiritual master, you persevered on the course and never became discouraged when there were delays in the attainment of success.

You were always fixed in the instructions of your spiritual master, and because your senses were perfectly controlled you were always constant in your determination to benedict the entire universe with his mercy. Even though there were sometimes seeming failures in your attempts, you were never sorry for that but carried in with determination and patience.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed the world what is a real *mahātmā:* you are always chanting Kṛṣṇa’s glories and endeavoring with great devotion.

The above descriptions of your determination are all found in your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* but this is only one of your divine qualities. I am reminded of how a friend described you to me when I first enquired about you. He said, “Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, he is a walking, talking *Bhagavad-gītā.”*

Of course, this homage has barely touched on one of your transcendental qualities, each of which is like a drop from the vast and unfathomable ocean of your glorious, divine attributes.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, if we, your disciples and granddisciples, can just develop one-billionth of your pure determination the whole world will be undoubtedly delivered to the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants in Sydney,*

## Tennessee Farm

*Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances eternally in the dust of your lotus feet. We who cannot live from day to day without your mercy—-how long, how many births will it take us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to realize that the priceless gift you have given us, the shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa, is the only answer to today’s problems and dangers?

One time when you were asked, “What does it mean by ‘lotus feet’? you gave this verse:

*Samāśritā ye pada-pallava-plavaṁ*  
*Mahat-padaṁ puṇya-yaśo murāreḥ*  
*Bhavāmbudhir vatsa-padaṁ paraṁ*  
*Paraṁ padaṁ yad vipadāṁ na teṣām*

**“For one has accepted the boat of the lotus feet of the Lord, who is the shelter of the cosmic manifestation and is famous as Mukunda or the give of** ***mukti,*** **the ocean of the material world is like the water contained in the hoofprint of a calf.** ***Paraṁ padam,*** **or the place where there is danger in every step of life.”**

In this material world, full of dangers and miseries, help us take shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet birth after birth.

Living at Murāri-sevaka we are constantly realizing how useless it is to try to endeavor on our own behalf without seeing Kṛṣṇa’s hand in everything. We realize you are molding us. Like a potter molds us by the fire of ordeal to become unconditionally surrendered to your lotus feet.

When we forget your lotus feet we are overcome by so many details and difficulties and fail at everything we try to do. Only by remembering your lotus feet and the perfect example of your life struggle (which Satsvarūpa dāsaGoswami has kindly given us) can we be successful in our fight against the material energy. Please make us like helpless, dependent children not knowing anything but the shelter of your lotus feet.

One time in Delhi you were asking one devotee why he didn’t want to stay in India. He gave so many reasons, and then you said to him, “Actually, you are not in India. Just like I am not here in India, or the United States, or Europe. I am actually ‘in Kṛṣṇa.’ Just like a small child, he can be at the ocean or in the mountains, but he is with his parents so he feels completely sheltered—-safe. But, he can be in his room, his own house, without his parents and he is distressed. So, I am in Kṛṣṇa, not here in the material world. So, you be in Kṛṣṇa, not in India!” We sometimes are forgetting our real identity. Please help us always be “in Kṛṣṇa,” in the invincible boat of His lotus feet.

Finally help us to develop Murāri-sevaka to be fully self-sufficient, only to fulfill your desire for the next phase of the movement. Before you left this mortal world you were going to show us how to manage these farm projects; now please direct our intelligence, take charge of our project. We are your fallen, dependent children and have no other shelter but your lotus feet. Mold us as you desire.

*Your dependent servants at Murāri-sevaka,*

## Tokyo

*Kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ādhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ‘nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Members of the Khasa races and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.”**

On five separate occasions the members of the Khasa race were blessed by Your Divine Grace. The preceding verse from your translation of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* makes special mention of the different races of the world, the Khasa race described as one with stunted growth of hair on the upper lip, i.e., Mongolians, Chinese, Japanese, etc.

The westernized islands of Japan were first honored by your presence in 1969. Your association was primarily with government officials; yet by the following year you had established the temple, and during the second visit you experienced the ease with which the Japanese willingly offered their obeisances, taking this as a positive indication of Japan’s ability to make advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You personally installed the Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities in 1972, and you were very pleased to see the number of Japanese attending.

There was a great disappointment for you on your fifth visit to Tokyo; you found the temple was about to be closed. It was your continuing order that Japan should have an active temple but regrettably the temple closed. The devotees left with your request to stay in Japan echoing in their ears and hearts.

We are so fallen and without any qualification whatsoever; yet through your causeless mercy you have given us and Nihon \[Japan\] the unending opportunity of shelter at your lotus feet.

In June of 1980 you so kindly arranged for the reestablishment of a Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa temple—*bhakti-yoga* center in this seemingly forgotten place, a place where impersonalism, voidism, and atheism are the top ranking “faiths” and where urban-industrial living has reached its apparent extreme. Now it is our only hope that we may please you and make up for so much time by putting forth your blissful and compassionate message to the mass of people who have no hope of being purified otherwise.

Gurudeva, you have given us clear instructions in how to bring the knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village, and by your boundless goodness of all races of the world can become purified simply by cultivating their association with your books and your devotees. By following in your lotus footsteps, we can only unceasingly refer to your writings and take every moment to display to everyone that there is simply no other shelter to be had. We repeatedly beg you with unfolded hands to allow us to continue taking the shelter of your sublime, unmotivated love.

Please avail all opportunities to us to be solely in your service, spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all conditioned souls.

*Your worthless servants, New Gayā Dhāma,*

Satyadeva dāsa adhikārī, Mahāguṇa-devī-dasi, Maitreya, Murāri, Hamāśṛñga / Śrī Caraṇam dāsa adhikārī, Mathurā-devī dāsī / Nāganātana dāsa brahmacari / Bhakta Philiip, Bhaktin Rinko, Jñānī-maya, Rāmacandra-manāḥ / Bhakta Willie, Bhaktin Eiko

## Toronto

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances. O Jagad-guru! Bold men of the past have conquered with physical feats which have put others to awe and wonder. When the first man to do so swam the English channel, it was considered a miraculous undertaking. When the mountain climber conquers Mount Everest or some courageous fellow barrel-rides Niagara Falls, the deeds are notes as incredulous. But, it is the opinion of those who have had your blessed association that your ability to place God-loving sentiments into the hearts of those whose cores of hearts were framed with steel is a task which has excelled all humanly-possible feats.

When we reflect back on our miscreant days of sinful activities and then imagine that we would undergo the transformation which we have undergone, we would call it nothing but visionary, unthinkable, and impossible. But such is the case. You have forced a golden age to permeate through the hard walls of Kali-yuga, the age of iron.

History also reveals that early developers of the twentieth-century transportation routes had constructed roads and railway systems by blasting through huge mountainous regions, and great bodies of water were spanned by massive bridges. The labor and achievements behind these ventures are no doubt remarkable.

You have also formed history because of your amazing spiritual force you have made Viakuṇṭha accessible by personally paving the golden road. You have personally blasted our huge mountains of sinful desire by the potency of Krsn’s holy name, and you have helped us to leap over the murky waters of the material ocean just as Hanumān leaped over the ocean by chanting “*Jaya* Rāma.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, great men of conviction and determination will always be remembered for their power. Acknowledging your undisturbed power, it wouldn’t be inappropriate to address you as the “lion” amongst great men (as the comparison is also given with Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu). When the lion roars and roars and then all the jungle creatures flee out of fear. Even the enormous elephant panics. When you vibrated your sound judgments you caused all the big demons, materialists, atheists, and agnostics to shake and shudder. Who would not marvel at such extraordinary force?

As your intimate disciples and granddisciples we may ask the inevitable question. What is the source of this power? Prahlāda Maharaja replies to the same question by saying the that this power comes from Lord Kṛṣṇa, and *Bhagavad-gītā* elaborates by saying that the more we surrender to His will, the more power is given to us.

Our humble request at New Remuṇā Dhāma is that you will not neglect to give us your unceasing mercy and enable this surrender to take place.

We pray, *Kṛṣṇa se tomāra, Kṛṣṇa dite pāro, tomāra śakati āche:* Kṛṣṇa is yours. You are able to give Him to us, for such is your power.”

*Your servants from New Remuṇā Dhāma in Toronto,*

## Trinidad

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the only solace and refuge in our lives. My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mind is always so fixed on Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, kindly shower your mercy on us so that we may attain to perfection, which is never to have any other desire but to fulfill your desires.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our intelligence is so full of faults, and our minds are so crazy; therefore, O my dear spiritual master, allow us to completely surrender to your glorious feet.

O my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, whatever we may possess we surrender unto you, and what do we have? We have this body and mind, but whatever we have we surrender to your glorious feet.

O my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in good or bad fortune, in life or at death, there is no other hope but taking shelter at your transcendental lotus feet.

O most beautiful, merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our father, mother, friend. You are our only shelter, our life and soul. You are everything to us although sometimes we suffer because of the three modes of material nature, which is the cause of our mind’s flickering and restlessness (*cañcalaṁ-saṅkalpa-vikalpa)* but your preaching is so strong that is the only thing we have to remind us that your desires are our life and soul and that Kṛṣṇa is the big boss and we are all His eternal servants and you our eternal spiritual master.

O master, please keep me and bless me so that I can render eternal loving service, void of motive. As Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu teaches us in His prayer, *Śrī Śrī Śikṣāṣṭaka:*

*Na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīṁ*  
*Kavitāṁ vā jagad-īśa kāmaye*  
*Mama janamani janmanīśvare*  
*Bhavatād bhaktir ahaitukī tvayi*

**“O almighty Lord, I have no desire to accumulate wealth, nor do I desire beautiful women, nor do I want any number of followers. I only want Your causeless devotional service, birth after birth.”**

As for me, I am begging you to bless me that I may never have any desire to accumulate wealth, beautiful women, or followers. Bless me so that all I want is to have your transcendental loving service. Please bless me in this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and forgive my volume of sins. Thank you my master.

*Your fallen but forever-begging servants at ISKCON Trinidad,*

## Vancouver

*Dearest Śrīal Prabhupada, ocean of mercy,*

With our insufficient brain substance we are struggling to fathom the infinite depths of the transcendental ocean of your exalted glorious qualities. We must make a confession, that for us devotional service is a most difficult task: blood, sweat, and tears. We are seeing devotees as ordinary men subject to so many faults. We are maintaining so many gross and subtle material attachments. We have no attachment for chanting the holy names. We have no affection for the Deity. We are so much lazy. We have no compassion for the conditioned souls in material existence. It is too embarrassing to continue in this way because we are so much neophyte and so much offensive. We are struggling to become devotees, even struggling to remain in the struggle to become devotees. It is a very, very difficult thing for us.

But somehow we are persevering. Somehow we are tolerating. Somehow we are becoming determined to go on in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is your divine grace, your causeless mercy. You are allowing us to appreciate a small particle of a drop from the ocean of your greatness. Savoring that particle of understanding, we are deriving the strength not only to carry on the struggle of our spiritual life but to smash and defeat all obstacles and impediments. When all else seems hopeless we take shelter in the greatness of Your Divine Grace. That…. “Śrīla Prabhupāda is such a glorious personality, and he is personally requesting that we do this!!”

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are begging that you allow us to understand your greatness and become attached to your lotus feet.

*Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya*  
*ār nā koriho mane āśā*  
*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei se uttama-gati*  
*je prasāde pūre sarve āśā*

**“Make the teachings from the lotus mouth of the spiritual master one with your heart, and do not desire anything else. Attachments to the lotus feet of the spiritual master is the best means of spiritual advancement. By his mercy all desires for spiritual perfection are fulfilled.”**

*Prasaṅgam ajaraṁ pāśam*  
*ātmanaḥ kavayo vidyuḥ*  
*sa eva sādhuṣu kṛto*  
*mokśa-dvāram apāvṛtam*

**“Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But with the same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of realization.”**

*Mahat-sevāṁ dvāram āhur vimktes*  
*Tamo-dvāraṁ yoṣitāṁ saṅgi –saṅgam*  
*Mahāntas te sama-cittāḥ praśāntā*  
*Vimanyavaḥ suhṛdaḥ sādhavo ye*

**“One can attain the path of liberation from material bondage only by rendering service to highly advanced spiritual personalities. These personalities are impersonalists and devotees. Whether one wants to merge into the Lord’s existence or wants to associate with the Personality of Godhead, one should render service to the** ***mahātmās.*** **For those who are not interested in such activities, who associate with people fond of women and sex, the path to hell is wide open. The** ***mahātmās*** **are equipoised. They do not see any difference between on e living entity and another. They are very peaceful and are fully engaged in devotional service. They are devoid of anger, and they work for the benefit of everyone. They do not behave in any abominable way. Such people are known as** ***mahātmās.”***

Thus it is confirmed that we must understand you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and become very much attached to you.

In your final pastimes in Vṛndāvana we witnessed how you very vividly taught this point. You were acting in such a way that our attachment for you would be intensified.

It was like Kṛṣṇa in the clutches of the Kāliya serpent. Although no one can defeat Kṛṣṇa, the supreme all-powerful Personality of Godhead, He allowed Himself to be captured for two hours like an ordinary child in the coils of Kāliya. Mother Yaśoda, Nanda Maharaja, and the residents of Vṛndāvana were filled with transcendental lamentation and anxiety. But this was Kṛṣṇa’s pastime to increase and intensify the bond of love between Himself and His devotee.

So also, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you put us in so much anxiety in your final pastimes. At one moment it seemed you would recover and remain with us, and at the next moment your departure seemed imminent. Back and forth your condition and mood fluctuated although it was entirely in your hands whether you were to stay or leave.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you even admitted that you were acting in this way so that we would increase our attachment for you.

We pray that we may always increasingly feel that attachment for you, that we may always feel an obligation to you. That….Śrīla Prabhupāda is so great, and he is personally asking us to do this!!

Thus by your divine grace we may be able to carry this burden of love. By your divine grace we may be able to tolerate happiness and distress, heat and cold, victory and defeat, honor and dishonor, pleasure and pain, and finally dedicate our selves cent percent to your divine instructions to bodily propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We are begging at your lotus feet for one drop from this ocean, for even one small particle of your mercy, your divine grace.

## Veracruz

Oh, My Lord and master for eternity, Śrīla Prabhupāda! We are assembled here today before your *vyāsāsana* in separation from your physical from (*vapuh)).* Some of us cry, others smile approvingly, and still others who can barely conceive anything at all of your magnanimity do not quite know how to react. Still we are trying to understand that your appearance and disappearance from its miserable world follow exactly in perfect order with the plan of Lord Kṛṣṇa to liberate the fallen and unfortunate souls such as I. Śrīla Prabhupāda, taking advantage of the very exalted quality of your service unto Him, Lord Kṛṣṇa has desired to magnify your work throughout the world. He Himself has empowered you with the holy name and the monumental treasure structured in the knowledge of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam.* We know well that no physical circumstance, desire or plan has caused you to descend to this material world. O *śaktyāveśa-avatāra!* Kṛṣṇa convinced you to come and deliver a few books, and you have done it. We know that it was your pure and sincere desire to serve Kṛṣṇa which made you descend. We cannot give any other explanation. During your sojourn in this world you were a genuine *ācārya.* By your own transcendental behavior you taught us to become determined in conquering the vain but strong temptations of *māyā,* walking firmly upon the sacred carpet of devotional service trodden upon by the *ācāryas.*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, your formidable example was in strictly following the orders of your Guru Maharaja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Gosvami Maharaja, with all the faith you possessed, due to the greatness of your devotional service, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bestow upon us that power of understanding so that we may serve you in the same way.

Who can describe the full activities of an *ācārya?* Only by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy are we able to describe with broken words your unlimited transcendental qualities.

The *guru* is not subject to material miseries, although he may appear to be. He is a *nitya-siddha,* an eternally liberated soul who descends to this material world to freely give Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.

My dear master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, personally all my aspirations to enjoy without you in this world are diminishing, and I am greatly repentant for all the time I wasted without appreciating what you were giving to me. Accept me eternally in your service. Engage me heavily in the preaching work. Help me to inject my heart cent percent with sincerity so that I may participate in the massive distribution of your monumental books. Allow me to associate eternally with your *vāṇī.*

*Humbly, your servants,*

Kṣiti-mohana dāsa brahmacari / Iśopaniṣad dāsa brahmacari / Akṣayānanda dāsa brahmacari / Ādi-kartā dāsa adhikārī / Lakṣmī-Nārāyaṇa dāsa adhikārī / Īśāna dāsa brahmacari / Bhakta Gustavo / Bhakta Rogelio / Bhakta Jose Luis / Bhakta Hector / Bhakta Roberto / Līlāvati-devī-dasi, Lakṣmaṇa / Siddhā-devī dāsī / Līlā-śakti-devī dāsī / Uttarā-devī dāsī / Gandhinī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Esmeralda

## Vienna

I offer my respectful obeisances at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who is very dear to Kṛṣṇa on this earth, because he has fully taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our obeisances unto you, the most confidential servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura. You are preaching the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and delivering the Western countries from impersonalism and voidism.

Eighty-five years ago in the holy land of Bhārata,  
In a holy family of pure Vaisnavas,  
The greatest saintly person of modern ages appeared.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you whose glories are sung  
Throughout the pages of Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,  
The unalloyed servant of the Supreme Lord,  
You have come and delivered the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you made your appearance in a confidential way,  
Just hours after Lord Kṛṣṇa’s appearance day,  
Making the dark sky of the upcoming century,  
Bright by your unlimited compassion and mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are eternally liberated—nitya-siddha  
Delivering the most fallen—kṛpā-siddha,  
Your pure and penetrating spiritual vision,  
Smashes the mountains of illusion,  
Just like Lord Indra’s thunderbolt,  
So bright, powerful, and bold.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are like rains of mercy  
Delivering the seeds of love and devotion,  
Your books are free from all imperfections,  
Your books are Lord Nityānanda’s expansions,  
Bestowing love of God on both the gentle and the ruffians,  
Defeating all rampant atheistic conceptions,  
Delivering us from the dungeon of material creation.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are tickets to Vaikuntha,  
Your books are messengers from Kṛṣṇaloka,  
You live eternally in your books,  
Your books are the life and soul of this lowly servant.

*Your servants in ISKCON center Vienna,*

## Vṛndāvana

Herald the day,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda has come  
To this world of misery,  
Vyāsa’s message for everyone.

Our master and guide,  
Deliverer of the world,  
In this age of Kali-yuga  
Caitanya’s flag unfurled.

Hard as a thunderbolt,  
Softer than a rose,  
Perfect judge of circumstance,  
Caitanya’s message disclosed.

Sent personally by his master  
To capture us from death,  
Our one and only master  
Siṁha-guru His Divine Grace.

Has he not come amongst us  
With message strong and bold,  
Our lives past in illusion  
Countless years untold.

O master please deliver us,  
Your mercy knows no bounds;  
We fall like dust beneath your feet,  
Free from māyā’s rounds.

Carry out your order,  
Recall the fallen souls,  
In every town and village  
Caitanya’s name our goal.

Your glory is unlimited  
Just like that of the Lord;  
Your message is for everyone,  
Attain the spiritual world.

Our life and soul belong to you,  
The greatest master and guide;  
Your words are molder of our life,  
To preach and turn the tide.

You’re here with us at every step  
To guide us in your books,  
But still we cry to hear your words,  
Prabhupada please instruct us.

Without your presence in our life  
Every moment of the day  
Our life is useless and unreal.  
Please guide us forever we pray.

## Washington D.C.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I offer my prostrated obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda, which award *prema-bhakti* to those who bathe in their dust. You are always maddened with ecstatic emotions from preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness; yet you are able to maintain your sober demeanor and regal stride. All glories unto you.

I have heard you were once asked to speak about your Guru Maharaja, and you replied, “What can I say about him? He was a Vaikuṇṭha man.” I in turn feel the same way about you. Though one may think the glorification of the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead becomes easier and easier in the course of time, I have not found this to be so. My experience is that as I go on in devotional service I gain more and more realization how the glories of a pure devotee are just as unfathomable and unlimited as the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself, and so I always find it a very humbling experience whenever I attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace.

“How can we best glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda?” When I ask this question of myself I always get the same answer: “By becoming a pure devotee.” My intelligence will not consider that there is any better way to please and glorify you. If we can become pure devotees then your teachings and glories will spread automatically, just as when you ( a pure devotee) came to the West the glories of your Guru Maharaja and the entire *paramparā,* which had therefore been unknown to us, became known. As your disciples, we want nothing more than to see your glories broadcast in every town and village all over the world. The best way to accomplish this is by becoming pure devotees. In other words, we must become living examples of all the things you stood for, because “example is better than precept.” This is certainly a fact. Unless we can become perfect examples of your precepts we will have failed to truly glorify you. This we do not want to do.

With this end in mind, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on behalf of all the devotees at New Hastināpura Dhāma and on my own behalf, I humbly submit myself at your lotus feet and taking firm hold of them I cry out for your rain of mercy upon us. Only by the mercy of a great personality like yourself can we give up our lusty desires for sense enjoyment and material opulence and replace them with pure desire to serve your lotus feet and the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana. Then, just like you, we too will have unflinching faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa; in the mission of Lord Caitanya; in the prophecy of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda; and in the order of our Guru Maharaja. In this way we will be able to convince the Washingtonians to take it Kṛṣṇa consciousness and make their lives auspicious. Thus the nation’s capital can be added to your growing list of conquests under the banner of Śrī Caitanya and His associates, and your glory as “the great soul who turned back Kali-yuga” will be acknowledged. This recognition you rightfully deserve. Please help us see you recognized in this way.

Jaya Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Abhaya Caraṇāvinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada Maharaja *ki jaya!*

*Your grateful disciples and granddisciples at Washington, D.C. ISKCON,*

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Australasia College Preaching Party

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

By your mercy we are engaged in trying to deliver the student community here. Along with the impersonalists, fruitive workers, blasphemers, false logicians and nondevotees, they are very expert in avoiding the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Just as Lord Caitanya devised many methods to liberate them, we pray at your lotus feet that you show us the ways and means to attract all these fools and rascals back to Godhead. And as you have written: “If we don’t recruit the intelligent class of men from the colleges, then we will simply go on expanding and eventually everything will collapse.”

So we are humbly desiring to do some tangible service in this connection but realize our pitiful inadequacies. Therefore, because we are shameless, we will request Your Divine Grace to please join our preaching party, and we will make all nice arrangements for you. We are convinced that even these students’ stone hearts can be melted if they get your all-attractive mercy in the form of books, *prasādam, kīrtana,* compassion and friendliness. Oh, what bliss it is! Feeling your presence in the remembrance and execution of your order, we will become like drops of nectar in your downpour of mercy on the conditioned souls. Please grant us this perfection life after life. We beg to remain,

*Your eager and willing servants,*

Dhṛṭadyumna Swami / Mahāśṛṅga dāsa brahmacari / Prahlāda-bhakta dāsa brahmacari / Bhakta Brad / Bhakta Vic

## Australian Bus Party

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Another year has passed since you were present before our vision, and although we try to remember you and your instructions throughout the year, at this particular time you very kindly and mercifully allow us to intensify our feelings for you. Thus we become submerged in an ocean of transcendental bliss and ecstasy.

As it is said in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:*

*Kathañcana smṛte yasmin*  
*Duṣkaraṁ sukaraṁ bhavet*  
*Vismṛte viparītaṁ syāt*  
*śrī-caitanyaṁ namāmī tam*

**“Things that are very difficult to do become easy to execute if one somehow or other simply remembers Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. But if one does not remember Him, even easy things become very difficult. To this Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu I offer my respectful obeisances.”**

This instruction also fully applies to remembrance of your divine self, Śrīla Prabhupāda—everything becomes clear and simple. But if we put those thoughts of yourself or your instruction aside, or if we minimize them, or if we deviate from them, then most certainly we’ll encounter a myriad of great difficulties. We pray to be able to follow your instructions implicitly, Prabhupada, and beg your mercy to this end.

How you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness has expanded, not just maintained, and as you were the force in the beginning, we should know positively that you are still the force now, giving us courage, strength, and enthusiasm. We are proud to be part of your ISKCON society, Prabhupada. It is not an ordinary or mundane society. Where is there another that can compare? All others are comprised of rogues, rascals, and cheaters, no matter what label is affixed to them.

We want to be able to give people your association, Prabhupada; we want them to know how wonderful you are, and we want that the whole world glorify you and realize who you are. Only in this way is there any hope or chance that they will be saved! Let them all chant along with us: *Jaya* Prabhupada! *Jaya* Prabhupada!

*Your menial servants,*

Rama dāsa brahmacari / Veṇugopāla dāsa brahmacari / Gaura Gopala dāsa brahmacari / Vrajarāja dāsa brahmacari / Nīlācala dāsa brahmacari / Akrūra dāsa brahmacari / Tīrtharaja dāsa brahmacari / Dharma dāsa adhīkārī

## Back to Godhead

The Bhagavad-gītā states that when religious practice declines and irreligious practice increases, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, appears. His mission is to lead people to God consciousness and obedience t the principles of religion. Sometimes He descends personally, and sometimes He sends His bona fide representative in the form of His sons or servants, who propagate the *dharma* of the age. In the present age this *dharma* is *sankirtana.* As the *Caitanya caritāmṛta* states.

*Kali-kālera dharma Kṛṣṇa-nāma-sankirtana*  
*Kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the congregational chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***sankirtana*** **movement.” (Cc.** ***Antya*** **7.11)**

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, should be considered an extraordinarily empowered servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Indeed, he is a *śaktyāveśa-avatara,* and this fact has been proved by his ability to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world. As the *jagad-guru,* the spiritual master of the entire world, he could deliver anyone back to Godhead. The teachings and example of such an empowered preacher must be followed by anyone who wants to understand transcendental knowledge and attain the goal of human life.

The greatest empowered servants of Kṛṣṇa are the twelve *mahājanas,* the leaders of devotional service. The activities and teachings of the *mahājanas* , some of whom are personal expansions of Kṛṣṇa Himself, are glorified in the Vedic epic *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,* which lists them as Lord Brahmā, Nārada Muni, Lord Śiva, the four Kumāras, Lord Kapiladeva, Prahlāda Mahāraja, Yamarāja, Janaka Maharaja, Śukadeva Gosvami, Bali Maharaja, Svāyambhuva Manu and Bhīṣmadeva. Śrīla Prabhupāda displayed many of the great qualities and abilities of the *mahājanas* in his preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world. Let us now look at how Śrīla Prabhupāda embodied some of the transcendental qualities of them.

**Lord Brahmā**

Śrīla Prabhupāda was the current representative of the disciplic succession coming down from Lord Kṛṣṇa to Lord Brahma, the original created being and the engineer of the material universe. Just as Lord Brahmā was empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa to create the material world, which gives the living entities an opportunity to use their allotted energies in the Supreme Lord’s devotional service, so Śrīla Prabhupāda was empowered by Kṛṣṇa to create the International Society for Krishna Consciousness to give people the opportunity to use everything to please Lord Kṛṣṇa. At the beginning of the creation, Lord Brahma prayed, “May the Supreme Lord be merciful toward me so that I, as before, may be empowered with the introspection to create, for I am also one of the surrendered souls who are dear to the Lord.” (*Bhāg.* 3.9.22) With the same devotional humility, Śrīla Prabhupāda, upon arriving in Boston harbor from India in 1965, prayed to Lord Kṛṣṇa for the spiritual strength to perform his service: “Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.”

**Narada Muni**

Since time immemorial N Muni has cleansed the hearts of conditioned souls with his singing of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s glories to the accompaniment of his *vīṇā.* Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda has attracted many people to Kṛṣṇa consciousness with his singing of transcendental songs to the accompaniment of *mṛdaṅga, karatālas,* or harmonium. The primary duty of those following in the line of Nārada Muni, Śrīla Prabhupāda said, is bringing the conditioned souls back to Godhead. N Muni gave shelter to a low-born hunter named Mṛgāri, but Śrīla Prabhupāda awakened Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the hearts of thousands of people addicted to sinful activities even more vicious than a hunter’s. Indeed, Mṛgāri hunted only animals in the forest, but now people kill without any restriction—babies in the womb, innocent cows, and even themselves. Śrīla Prabhupāda is saving such lost souls.

Misguided materialists cannot recognize the greatness of saints like Śrīla Prabhupāda and N Muni. Dakṣa cursed N to have no fixed residence because he had converted thousands of Dakṣa’s sons to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda said some of his disciples’ parents had also cursed *him* to have no fixed residence. Śrīla Prabhupāda assures us, however, that because the curses against N and him resulted in traveling and preaching for Kṛṣṇa consciousness, these “curses” were actually benedictions. But Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted to transfer the curse to his young disciples, particularly his *sannyāsīs,* so that he could sit in one place and translate *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam.*

**Lord Śiva**

Maharaja Dakṣa also previously cursed Lord Śiva, during a sacrifice attended by all the principal demigods, but Lord Śiva remained undisturbed and continued his meditation on Lord Viṣṇu. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda had to endure many offenses from politicians, false incarnations, and ordinary demons, but he remained unaffected. Sometimes, simply by his charm and composure, Śrīla Prabhupāda would dissipate the hostility and doubts of his challengers. Just as Lord Śiva possesses all material wealth but lives beneath a tree, Śrīla Prabhupāda was the leader of a prosperous international movement but preferred to live simply. We should also note that both Lord Śiva and Śrīla Prabhupāda are so compassionate that they give shelter and their transcendental association to many low-class, ghostly living beings to elevate them to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Now people who were formerly immersed in the modes of ignorance and passion are healthy, happy, and progressing back to Godhead.

**The Four Kumāras**

Being completely absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda always appeared youthful and effulgent, just as the four Kumāras always appear youthful and bright by their mystic power. Those under the modes of material nature cannot understand the Vaisnavas; therefore great Vaisnavas like the four Kumāras and Śrīla Prabhupāda are usually invisible to ordinary persons, although such Vaisnavas travel all over the universe to liberate the conditioned souls. But Śrīla Prabhupāda was even more merciful than the Kumāras; although the Kumāras are reluctant to go to sinful places, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the order of his spiritual master, came to the most sinful place, the Western countries, to purify the people there.

**Lord Kapiladeva**

Both Lord Kapildeva and Śrīla Prabhupāda propagated pure devotional service; so those who know the transcendental purpose of human life consider them the most important personalities. In the last years of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s manifest presence, at an age when most men had retired, he traveled extensively all over the world to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. During the twelve years of his most far-reaching preaching, Śrīla Prabhupāda personally guided many thousands of disciples, through lectures, books, letters, tapes, and his personal association. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s very life was preaching, and through his preaching he gave back to the real life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to many conditioned souls.

**Prahlāda Maharaja**

Like Prahlāda Maharaja, Śrīla Prabhupāda remembered Lord Kṛṣṇa despite the greatest difficulties. Although living in the hellish Bowery in New York, Śrīla Prabhupāda was unconcerned about danger; he was always confident of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s protection. Absorbed in remembrance of Lord Kṛṣṇa from the very beginning of their lives, both Prahlada and Śrīla Prabhupāda always worshiped Him with enthusiasm. Even the opposition of their family members could not dissuade them from their service to the Lord; instead, they converted the sons of many demons to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Without a thought for any material benefit, they underwent great tribulations simply out of compassion for the fallen, conditioned souls.

**Yamarāja**

Yamarāja was so eager to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness that he willingly accepted the position of a *śūdra,* Vidura. Śrīla Prabhupāda also underwent many hardships, such as crossing the turbulent waters of the Atlantic Ocean to preach in a foreign land where he had no connections and no apparent means of support.

**Janaka Maharaja**

As Janaka Maharaja brought forth the goddess of fortune even from the dust, Śrīla Prabhupāda founded the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement amidst the dirt of materialistic America.

**Śukadeva Gosvami**

Śukadeva Gosvami’s father, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, is the literary incarnation of God, and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s spiritual father, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Thākura, was the foremost scholar and devotee of the recent age. By the mercy of the Supreme Lord and the great spiritual masters, both Sukadeva Gosvami and Śrīla Prabhupāda narrated the *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* for the enlightenment of the conditioned souls in Kali-yuga.

**Bhīṣmadeva**

Bhīṣmadeva, the great devotee and soldier, fought fiercely on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra although, he had long since earned the title “Grandfather Bhīṣma.” Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although in his seventies, worked so strenuously at preaching, translating, and traveling around the world that even his youthful disciples could not keep up with him. As the time of departure approached, both Bhīṣmadeva, lying on a bed of arrows, and Śrīla Prabhupāda, afflicted by painful disease, spoke perfectly on religious principles according to time, place and audience. Thus Lord Kṛṣṇa proved that his pure devotees are always sound in body and mind by dint of spiritual enlightenment. Lord Kṛṣṇa personally attended the passing away of both Bhīṣmadeva and Śrīla Prabhupāda. Those present when Bhīṣmadeva quit his material body could see Lord Kṛṣṇa standing in His four-armed form before the great warrior, and those present at the time of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s departure experienced Lord Kṛṣṇa’s presence in the incarnation of the holy name.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s delivering the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the most auspicious event in the history of the West. He is like an eternal sun, illuminating everything with the brilliance of his preaching. Owl-like men who choose to close their eyes to his effulgent teachings will remain perpetually in darkness. But those who take advantage of the opportunity to become God conscious will surely attain the ultimate goal of life and return back to the spiritual kingdom.

Satsvarūpa dāsaGosvami / Jayādvaita Swami / Yogeśvara dāsa / Ravīndra-svarūpa dāsa / Prahlādānanda dāsa / Draviḍa dāsa / Yamaraja dāsa / Jaya Jagadīśa dāsa / Tattvavit dāsa / Taponidhi dāsa / Ṛkṣaraja dāsa / Sādhana-siddhi dāsa

## FATE (East)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, a moment’s association with a pure devotee is an inestimable gift. Your association is the personification of that moment. It allows us to watch the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhagavatam* come to life. Without your supplying us with the vital example of practical application, spiritual life would still be two words in a *yoga* book for most of us. My association with you personally was only for moments at a time, but when I think back on those moments they have been etched into my consciousness like the lines on my palms.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given the other devotees at FATE and myself a lifetime’s engagement of illustrating with dioramas the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We are just beginning to scratch the surface of the results of this engagement, and we pray that they are pleasing to you. You have also promised to help us with ideas in our humble attempts to create new dioramas; without your help it is an impossible task.

To purify our practical engagement and to regulate it, you have given us a wonderful style of living which allows us to see through the clouds of illusion which beset any endeavor in this material world. Because there is danger at every step and because we want very much to create for you something that you will be proud to offer to Lord Kṛṣṇa, we, the devotees at the First American Transcendental Exhibition, wish to fall prostrated at your lotus feet and take shelter there permanently. You have every right to kick me away because of my offensive mentality towards those whom you have inspired to help with this project, but I pray that you will allow this floundering servant to remain in some capacity of service to you eternally.

In meetings with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and in letters, you have mapped out a very ambitious course for the devotees here. You have asked that there be exhibits installed in every one of your centers all over the world—an order which is ever-expanding with the growth of ISKCON. Please allow us to endeavor to our full capacity and offer all the results to you and your bona fide representatives.

*Your fallen servants,*

The devotees of the First American Transcendental Exhibition

## Friends of Lord Kṛṣṇa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Rama. Please accept our obeisances. All glory to your appearance by which the light and knowledge from Kṛṣṇa has manifest itself in this age. We cannot repeat the great words of praise and realizations of so many exalted personalities. We can only thank you for giving this chance to participate in this movement founded by Lord Caitanya. You have given us the chance to gain the most sought after gift of all. Thank you for bringing the Hare Kṛṣṇa *maha-mantra* to us.

*Jaya* Rādhe! *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants,*  
Friends of Lord Kṛṣṇa  
Sahadeva-sakhā dāsa and family

## Friends of Kṛṣṇa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my respects. In late 1977 you wrote me a letter requesting that I go ahead and print a newsletter for all the parents and relatives of devotees. With the encouragement of Rāmeśvara Swami and the assistance of my good husband, this has been possible. I am happy to say that my mailing list has now grown to include more than 2,500 people, and the newsletter comes out regularly every two months.

Many, many parents have corresponded with me over the past four years, and I have developed many nice friendships from this correspondence. I am very grateful to you for giving me the opportunity to help other parents of devotees accept and appreciate their children’s new way of life. Also, at times I have been able to help the devotees become more understanding and communicative with their parents.

Unfortunately, it is not uncommon nowadays to split apart because of economic, social, and even religious reasons. However, the family unit is an integral part of life, and it is my hope that by providing factual information about Kṛṣṇa consciousness to people everywhere, our project can help to end the troubles experienced by families when religious differences arise.

*Sincerely,*  
Friends of Kṛṣṇa  
Sam and Rose Forkash

## Reverend Father J.W.S John

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I wonder at your thoughts, wisdom and possibilities; for you sent your *svāmī* from Mombasa Hare Kṛṣṇa temple to come and open the Hare Kṛṣṇa temple in Kisumu a the foot of the hill on Kakamega Road within the Holy Coptic Church.

The wisdom and power with which you took your words from India to the West (America), we pray to also have in order to give your message to the people of Kisumu. We worship and glorify you, O our beloved *guru.* Make the words Hare Kṛṣṇa not step out of our mouth and mind. Move us slowly and peacefully until we manage to build the Kisumu temple the way it is needed to be, as you have done in so many countries.

Oh, Lord Kṛṣṇa, You love us so much You have taught us Your true words from Your book *Bhagavad-gītā.* Now please surround Lake Victoria and hold it with both left and right hands like someone carrying a bowl, and make the people around Lake Victoria know Your words, which have been hidden from Africa for so long. Let them shine like moonlight which shines very softly. Through Your unlimited power let us Africans, who did not know You, learn about You. Let the light of His Divine Grace Swami Prabhupada shine like the light that shines in the darkness. I know it is You have the power to hold the thunderstorms, lightning, earthquakes, rains, and even the sun during the droughts. Bring us peace and love to You.

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare.*

*Your servant,*  
H.H Pro. Rev. Father J.W.S. John  
(Spiritual leader of Holy Ghost Coptic Church of Africa St. Phillip Dela Neri; Kisumu, Kenya)

**Part 1**

All hail the glorious one,  
All hail our divine master,  
Father and friend,  
Blessed and dearmost servant of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya  
Oṁ Viṣṇupada Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya  
Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad  
His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada,

Who suffered the rebukes of fools  
Who could not understand the need for his writing,  
His necessary break from his family  
Who answered them:  
What has this family given me? Why  
Should I forget about God? This is the real  
Thing, what I am doing. You cannot  
Realize what I am doing.

Who founded the League of Devotees at Jhansi  
Who quoted Caitanya-caritāmṛta  
Who told shopkeeper / friend:  
The whole world is waiting, Mr.Mitra,  
For our spiritual revolution.

Who laughed when his Allahabad business  
Was burglarized; a loss of 7,000 rupees,  
Then uttered the Bhagavata verse spoken by his Guru Maharaja  
Yasyāham anugṛhṇāmi  
Hairṣye tad-dhanaṁ śanaiḥ  
Tato ‘dhanaṁ tyajanty asya  
Sva-janā duḥkha-duḥkhitam

“When I feel especially merciful towards  
someone, I gradually take away all his material  
possessions. His friends and relatives then  
reject this poverty-stricken and most  
wretched fellow.”

Who losing the League’s buildings  
Left for Mathurā  
Carrying a Deity of Lord Caitanya.

Who wished Sajjana-toṣaṇī  
To come to the standard of India’s  
Illustrated Weekly,  
But did not have even one good dhotī  
To meet subscribers, to preach.

Who by the arrangement of Śrī Kṛṣṇa  
(Who had a greater plan)  
could not secure the cooperation  
of his Godbrothers.  
Who alone, without money or shelter  
By his great mercy  
Graced the homes of Delhi.

Who, in 1952, revived Back to Godhead  
Alone, collecting funds, writing,  
Overseeing, printing and distributing  
One thousand copies.

Whose friend, a Delhi Supreme Court Judge  
Would later remember:  
“It was only a four-page magazine,  
but it showed his study of the subject and  
his earnestness and devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa.  
The only thing that impressed one and  
Was worth noticing at the time was the  
Simplicity of his abiding faith in God’s name  
And in His mission.”

Who wrote the president of India  
Of his plan to take the world back to Godhead.  
Who begged for a ministry of spiritual affairs  
Who admitted:  
“I am crying in the wilderness at the present moment.”

Who “was forced “ by his Guru Maharaja  
(through dreams and Godbrother̮  
to take sannyāsa in Vṛndāvana.  
Who translated Śrīmad-Bhagavatam,  
Who at sixty-nine, overcoming extreme difficulties  
Fulfilled the order of His Guru Maharaja  
Given forty years before.  
Who on August 13,1965,  
Alone and full of hope  
Boarded the Jaladuta in Calcutta.

## SACE

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You said that people would first laugh at us, then they would persecute us, and then love us. By your mercy you have given the SACE devotees, among others, a little look into the future, a glimpse of what it will be like when people love us.

You always wanted traveling festivals. The spiritual world is a constant round of festivals, and you come from there, so it’s no wonder you always wanted festivals, one after another, every day of the year. You said it, and wished it, and not it has incredibly come true. Upwards of two million have attended South Asian Cultural Exhibition festivals, and not less than 100,000 have purchased *prasādam* within the last eighteen months.

We could tell more or less the same story for each of the forty college campuses (average size twenty thousand students) we have visited. Because all living entities are part and parcel of God and Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not an artificial imposition, they become excited as soon as they receive our brochure in the mail, they convene a meeting to allot the finances and set a date.

Two nights before the festival our caravan of huge trucks, trailer, and mobile camper arrives at the campus security gate. We are escorted by the police to prearranged parking spaces with electric and water hook-up.

Early next morning the set-up crew of five men, headed by Madhuhā dāsa, goes into action. Students are amazed at the efficiency, enthusiasm, and coordination of these men. These are not ordinary workers, but Vaisnavas, transcendental to the material modes. By noon we need help for putting up the four center poles of the big top tent. Within five minutes after going off, Madhuhā returns with six weight lifters from the gym. That done, fifty beautifully decorated panels (four feet by six feet) depicting every aspect of Kṛṣṇa consciousness snap into place on their chrome poles. When the full size “Changing Bodies” exhibit emerges from the truck, it immediately attracts a prefestival crowd. Out come the scale model temples, weapons, and musical instruments. Ten gorgeously dressed mannequins are unveiled. Deity clothes are mounted behind plexiglass, and the Bengali hut comes together piece by piece.

Night falls over the campus, which is already buzzing with the news of the colored tents and tomorrow’s festival. There is no despondency on campus tonight, for tomorrow you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will deliver them all from taking birth in the lower species of life next time around. You have said that anyone who in any way even sympathizes with the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement comes back with human form. Meanwhile the SACE men must guard the valuable equipment and take turns standing watch.

Before daylight a small blue Vega pulls up to a half-hidden, rear entrance to the cinema tent. Out steps Cārurūpa, the cook, who takes a look in either direction to make sure the coast is clear, and starts furtively shuttling huge bags of potatoes, cases of cauliflower, and cartons of milk into the back of the tent. The cut-up begins, the stoves are fired, and soon the aroma of *prasadam* impregnates the morning air. Before the campus rears its head and shakes off the drones of sleep, Cārurūpa has done it again. Śrīla Prabhupāda, not less than fifty gallons of curd *sābjī* and forty gallons of turmeric rice are miraculously ready for serving, and thousands of cauliflower heads are ready to be made into *pakorās* on the spot.

Man the steam table, start the movies…BBT artist in place? …Sewing demonstration ready? Two new machines, “Bhakta Jet Spray” and “Bhakta Taylor,” stand in place, ready at the flick of a wrist to dispense orange *lassīs* and strawberry *kolfi* ice cream, respectively. By ten o’clock word has gotten around about Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *prasadam,* the cafeteria empties even of its own employees, and we’re swamped. *Lakṣmī* rolls in, both at the food table and at the table where books, posters, *kurtās,* tapes, and other paraphernalia are sold.

The entertainment arrives. Either Chitresh, a Bengali *kathak* dancer, dazzles everyone with the footwork, or Śatarūpā dance a sublime pastime of Vraja with Prajāpati narrating. A play, *The Butcher’s Nightmare,* advocates cow protection.

By the time everyone’s attention has turned to the elephant. Dixie, who steps daintily out of her thirty-foot trailer and willingly begins to assume riders.

Twilight comes. Students drift off to campus. Our men start the arduous take-down. Tomorrow they have to set up at another campus. Tomorrow groups of students will gather on the empty squad and relive the day’s experiences. “Did they check out that elephant?...That food was out of this world….they said it was something cooked in ghee….They has this far out Indian hut over here with sculptures in it, and over there was an art piece of reincarnation….Did you know that every cell in your body changes every seven years?”

By now your disciple Madhuhā is beyond material pains and pleasures. As he so often does, he forgoes sleep and ignores an aching back in order to pack up the trucks. Cārurūpa, although black from head to foot from cooking *pakorās* all day long, scrubs down pots by flashlights. When everyone gets up tomorrow morning at about 6.00 A.M. he will lead an ecstatic *maṅgala-āarti* on harmonium and give a short *Bhagavatam* class, to the point:

“These festivals are what Prabhupada wanted. He wanted the whole world to be happy. Every day should be a new festival, just like in Vṛndāvana. Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted people to be become lovers….lovers of Kṛṣṇa…lovers of *Kṛṣṇa-prasadam….*and lovers of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees. ….Love is our life…our real life…..Cultural conquest….Prabhupada’s is like a spiritual Genghis Khan. Wherever he and his armies of devotees go, the weeds of material life have trouble growing again. …Work now, *samādhi* later.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, wherever we see happy people we cannot but think of how you single-handedly brought this to the Western countries, of how you alone in the whole world know the art of making people permanently happy through transcendental knowledge. We’d like to thank you with all our hearts for enlightening our dark souls by delivering the all-attractive Supreme Personality of Godhead to us. But we’d like to thank you even more, a million times more, for ordering us to deliver it to others. Give us the vision to always keep order weighing on our backs, weighing so heavily in fact that we continue on and on. You gave your life unsparingly in doing welfare work for others. We aspire to follow in your footsteps. Let us no longer know what personal happiness or unhappiness is. Give us your mercy so that we may be only happy when others are happy, and that we may be unhappy when others are unhappy. Your mercy is all that we are made of, and if you do not continue to be merciful to us, we cannot maintain our lives.

*South Asian Cultural Exhibitions*
